- **LLP 1109's three questions wait on Charlie** (2026-10-08, [LLP 1109](llp/1109-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-exact2.rfc.md), app farm round 1): D1 `now()` as the date on every host (recommended; 12 builds wrote 1970 deadlines), D2 keep refusing an answer's undeclared field now that it is loud (or drop it and say so), D3 a failure's reason in the view (not yet). Build what is accepted.
- **A drive's page can lose a lazy chunk when another build replaces the output mid-drive** (2026-10-08, app farm round 1, territory-mapper-0429: the dev loop's watcher and a `test web` rebuild broke a hashed import). Concurrent builds no longer mix (`replaceBuild`, `host/web/serve.mjs`), but the agent's web carrier serves `target/web-dist` live, so a page booted from one build asks for `[name]-[hash].js` that a newer build with other inputs no longer has. Serve a drive from the build it booted (a copy, or the replaced tree kept until the drive ends).
- **Native Fixture's header segment tap is unavailable under `--chrome platform`** (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 S4s drive): `tap segment-missed` answers `native segment #15 is unavailable`. `AgentIOS.tap` asks the content `SegmentHost` before `NavigationHost.activateChrome`; the header's segmented title view lives on its route controller instead. The authored-chrome drive and the title-view's UIKit value-change XCTest pass. Route agent activation to the header projection; this was found during verification and has not been attributed to step 4.
- **The wasm page drops a video's opening duration under load** (2026-10-07, video-player conformance, about 1 run in 9 on main with 37 failures: `slots.duration` wasm 0, js 10, the remaining time `-0:00` for the session). `host/web/glue.js`'s media send drops any report that arrives before `inputReady` (the data executor); the opening `durationchange` (and `loadedmetadata`, `canplay`, `ended`, `error`) can fire first and never comes again. Branch `x2/vp-conform` (on origin) holds two attempts and five review rounds (`llp/reviews/code-2026-10-07-video-player-conformance*.md` on that branch): replaying waiting reports, then reporting the element's facts once the host can; each round found newer state a late report could overwrite (a source the host wrote before the glue applied it, a seek, a replayed press, a second load). Likely right fix: attach the media glue (`installMedia`) only once the host takes reports, so its existing attach-time block reports the element as it is then; the cost is that autoplay and bound media props apply only at readiness on a page rendered before its data executor.

"grid" : "block")` keeps its first width. - **Keyboard, the rest of #140** (2026-10-07, after `keyup`, `KeyboardEvent.code` and `.repeat` landed). Two parts wait on the owner's word: a capture-phase handler and its spelling (`keycapture=…`, ancestors outermost first, `stopPropagation()` keeping the key from the target), and a reserved held-modifier fact (`exactKeyboard`, reset on window blur, beside `keyup`; the web's way, keyup plus `exactPage().hasFocus`, is documented). A ⌘ chord during a composition now commits it and reaches the `key` handlers on macOS and iOS; not yet pressed with a real Korean or Japanese input source. Composing keys still differ by host: the web host hands every key Chrome sends while composing to `key` handlers (Contract has no `isComposing`, so a composer's Enter there can send mid-syllable), the native hosts keep them for the input method; and AppKit reports composed text to `input` only once committed, where Chrome reports it as it is composed. Real keys (`orca computer`, events posted to the app's pid, macOS 26.6.2) gave Exact and Chrome 154 the same log: ⌘B is keydown Meta, keydown b, keyup b (with ⌘ still held), keyup Meta. A held key's auto-repeat is not yet pressed for real: the CLI cannot hold a key, and auto-repeat downs posted to a pid reached neither app; a keyboard by hand, or a HID-level post with the app frontmost, would show it. iOS reports no `repeat` (UIKit's presses carry none) and the terminal host sends no `keyup`.

- **`text-decoration-color` and `text-underline-offset`** (2026-10-06, the concept of #75 by Jon Samp, whose branch main outran; its third row, `cursor`, landed in c6136f39d and f7e010921). Two CSS longhands as schema rows: `text-decoration-color` not inherited, `currentcolor` by default; `text-underline-offset` inherited, `auto` by default. The `text-decoration` shorthand takes a colour, which `shorthands.rs` refuses today because native painters draw only solid `currentcolor`. Draw them natively rather than admitting web-only rows: Apple's `underlineColor`/`strikethroughColor` (an offset needs a drawn underline, or a named refusal; `Text.swift` is near the cap), Linux's `paint/text_decoration.rs`, the terminal's SGR 58. The line takes the decorating box's colour, as CSS does.
- **iPhone Duo: navigation bar items under the status capsule** (2026-10-06, the concept of #90 by Jon Samp). UIKit keeps a navigation controller's vertical-bar items clear of the status bar and camera only when no scroll view sits above the controller's view; Exact's controllers sit inside the viewport, a scroll view, so on the Duo native-fixture's badge item and the detail screen's Back sit under the capsule and taps miss them. #90 moved the controllers to a stage beside the viewport, which paints them over later siblings and the popover top layer, against LLP 1083.000. Try a safe-area correction on the controllers first (`additionalSafeAreaInsets` from the window's), keeping tree order; verify with `smoke.mjs duo` and shop F21 / recipes F23.
- **Server-render CPU per page** (2026-10-06, the concept of #81, which main outran; the web-framework-bench's weakest result, 3–21× the best competitor). Re-port on main: skip pre-compressing `dist/.gen` at bind and the number-text fast path (both cherry-pick clean from #81); one site index per plan shared across requests (`runner.rs` is at the cap); a decoded constant pool for `vm.rs`, without #81's load-and-field fusion, which changes the machine `Vm.lean` proves; re-profile the per-template style memo after StyleProps' shrink (71792e51d). Measure on a quiet machine and run the theme-flip test #81 skipped. Also seen: the render server serves `dist/.gen` (intermediates and the plan's source map) to anyone; `static_file` refuses no hidden path, and `/.exact/` must keep working.
- **web-js: 10,000-row list updates** (2026-10-06, the concept of #82, LLP 1071.000, goal approved 2026-09-30). Every commit scans the page for `[navigationBack]`, about 40% of an update's script at 10k rows; the emitter can say a plan has no navigation roots. Then: a clone cache in `h()` keyed by the whole site, text bindings writing `Text.data`, one write per `css()`, a single-element move, and the data seam's per-row conversion (re-check #82's value-to-type memo against streamed answers, `ts-stream.js`, which mutate). `rt.js` (1,499 lines) and `emit.rs` (1,495) are at the cap. #82's grid numbers: update every 10th of 10k rows 537–1,039 ms against Svelte's 442.
- **`exact release` signs a Mach-O asset after the bake hashed it** (2026-10-06, Grok's review of #199). The release signs every Mach-O under `Contents/Resources` (so a helper in `assets/` notarizes), but the bake recorded those files' digests first: an update check stages the origin's pre-signing bytes, and a later generation runs that copy, not the signed one. Sign such files before their digest is recorded, or keep code out of the content-addressed assets. Also: a directory in `assets/` named like a bundle (`foo.bundle`) but holding no `Info.plist` is now signed as one, and `codesign` refuses it.
- **Wake-driven activation has no end-to-end test** (2026-10-06, reviews of #193). Its tests fire the wake by hand: nothing checks that a loader finishing alone wakes Linux's display loop or Apple's session and presents the activated frame. And `native::when_loaded` treats a missing digest or a poisoned lock as loaded (the retry then asks `preload` again).
- **A newer `minimumOS` still meets hardcoded module targets** (2026-10-06, after #64). The macOS Swift link now follows the manifest, but the module dylib's macOS `-target` is `macos14.0` and the iOS device target `ios17.0` in `build.mjs`; an app at 26 linking a 26-built xcframework into its module could be refused there.
- **A camera module** (2026-10-02, Charlie lifted "No camera anything", `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Components). An app native module in LLP 1067.000's shape, as `apps/recorder` is: `<camera-preview>` and `start`/`stop`/`capture`/`flip` on one object per session, `capture` writing to `app:/camera/`, the `device.camera` grant, a deterministic agent substitute (and a preview `snapshot` drawn from the last frame, since a capture layer is absent from `drawHierarchy`). Web, iOS and macOS first. Needs its RFC with an implementer and a date; open question: whether a camera can be shared across apps while an app has one module.
- **iOS: a pressable glass button misses real touches over a transform drag's handle** (2026-10-06, the Bluesky clone's lightbox, b08 and b09). Close and menu buttons with `backgroundMaterial="glass"`, siblings painted over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle (`press=tap`, `dblclick`), took no HID tap on the iOS 27 simulator (`axe touch`): the tap toggled the handle's `press` instead. The agent's taps reached them, and with the material removed the same HID tap pressed the button. Interactive glass returns its effect's `contentView` from `hitTest` (`NodeViewIOS.swift`, "Interactive glass … answers a finger itself") and relies on the responder chain reaching the node; a recognizer on the effect or the sibling handle probably takes the touch first. Reproduce with a glass button over a handle in a fixture and an XCTest real touch, then fix.
- **A timer overdue at a gesture's event starts its transitions at the event's time** (2026-10-06, Grok's r2 review of `bsky/release-clock`). `advance_for_input` fires the timers due by a release or geometry at their own runner times, but the hold update (or geometry's `engine.advance`) has already moved the motion engine to the input's time, so a transition a timer starts begins there, not at its due time (`Engine::observe` starts at `self.now`). Only a timer the host's own wake missed is overdue at an input, so the shift is normally under a frame; committing the timers' receipts before the hold or geometry seeks the engine would remove it.
- **Apple resize handlers run at the runner's last advance** (2026-10-06, the review of `bsky/release-clock`, rounds 1–2). `resize_rounds` (`host/apple/src/resize.rs`) dispatches with `runner.dispatch`, so a handler's `now()` and an `after` it arms are the runner's last advance, which a motion tick or a height hold leaves behind the host's clock; and a window resize reaches the runtime without its time (`Session.swift` `runtime.resize`, `host.rs` `resize`), so even the host's clock is old after an idle spell. Advancing inside the rounds is not enough: the timers' receipts must be laid out before the round gathers its notifications and moves its depth threshold (Astra r2). Carry the observation's time through the resize bridge and advance before the viewport settles.
- **Linux retained press/swipe and the terminal's keys still run at the runner's last advance** (2026-10-06, the review of `bsky/release-clock`, which moved the Apple and web gesture releases and geometry to the input's time). Linux `dispatch_retained` (`host/linux/src/host.rs`) calls `dispatch_bound` and sets `now_ms` after; the terminal dispatches a key after a poll of up to 250 ms that followed its advance (`host/terminal/src/term.rs`). An action's `now()` and an `after` it arms are then that old; advance first, keeping Linux's rule that a refused binding takes neither the sample nor an ordinary action.
- **Android start from the launcher's icon: exact2's content shows first** (2026-10-06, android/xicon on f3347f0a6, reader e6ef5c7; Pixel 10 Pro XL, five warm launches each by a tap on the app's icon in the all-apps list, `~/bench/android/results/video/xicon-1`, recorder `results/xblank/tools/record-icon.sh`). The icon grows into the window over about half a second (settled 530-560 ms for every app; `startvid.py`'s "visible", the app's ink at its final place, 290-380 ms, is that animation's progress), white until the app's own frame is in it. Content first seen inside the growing window (the white window's pixels turning to content, `xicon-1/seen.json`), medians: daily exact2 136 ms, Views 162, Compose 163; Extra Heavy exact2 161, Views 199, Compose 233; the content frame's present in the trace 100 / 124 / 129 and 122 / 157 / 185. So the earlier frame is seen 26-38 ms sooner here, where `am start`'s cross-fade hid it. The end of the animation is the launcher's, not the app's or its theme's.
- **Android start as seen: the grey wash was the platform's focus highlight; nth-C's Extra Heavy needs the phone unlocked** (2026-10-06, android/xstart; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/video/xstart-1`, five warm launches each; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xstart/reader-xstart.patch` against the bench reader's fce7c67). The daily app's window looked grey to the end of a recording, not for the cross-fade's last 25 ms: its view is focusable (keys), a launch by `am start` leaves the window out of touch mode, and Android draws its default focus highlight over the whole focused view (paper 223 for 254). With `defaultFocusHighlightEnabled = false` (the app draws its own focus): content visible 201-231 -> 187-195 ms (Views 183-190, Compose 180-195), settled 283-306 -> 287-301 (281-295, 288-304); xheavy unchanged, 216-224 / 306-310 (Views 210-222 / 302-314). exact2's content frame is still first (daily 78-96 ms against 107-113, xheavy 95-107 against 133-149), but what shows is the system's cross-fade from the launch, the same for every app: an earlier frame inside it shows no sooner by `am start`. Not built: the app's own task-open animation (a theme's window animations) would shorten what `am start` shows and nothing a launcher's own icon animation does. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (nth-C) v8's Extra Heavy fling never ran on exact2 or Views: the phone had restarted and not been unlocked (`dumpsys user`: RUNNING_LOCKED), and MapLibre's `FileSource` reads SharedPreferences from credential-encrypted storage in an AsyncTask, which throws until the first unlock and takes the process down as the first map row is made (Compose makes its map later). Unlocking the phone once clears it; making the apps survive it means redirecting MapLibre's preferences and files to device-protected storage in each app (it resolves the application context itself).
- **Android: Extra Heavy's 24k blank, its causes, and the measurements still owed** (2026-10-06, android/xblank on android/explore 1ac9a73e3; Pixel 10 Pro XL; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xblank/reader-xblank.patch` against the bench reader's aaf2f06, tools in `results/xblank/tools`). Measured on diagnostic builds over 9c8ccbead, one to five runs each; **the before/after tables on the final builds (xheavy, heavy, crypto; blank, fps, CPU) were not taken: the phones' host dropped off the tailnet (its node key expired)**; `results/xblank/tools/measure.sh` runs them (`*-exactxb0` before, `*-exactxb` after, APKs in `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-xblank/apk`). Found with a frame tag (`tag=1`: the reader draws each frame's number in the header and logs its feed's rows, `tagjoin.py` joins a recording's blank frames to them and to a trace): (1) `blank.py` took the launch animation for the driver's start in some runs, so their "24k" window measured 12k: every "bimodal" low reading (crypto's 0.3% baseline, xheavy's 0.5%) was that; fixed in `~/bench/android` ea94a6e. (2) `cover=1` read "covered" because a row's recorded bounds were its boxes unclipped: the list's spacer for unmounted rows is a row over a million px tall that draws nothing, and a horizontal list's content box 239,160 px wide; fixed here (each box within its clip, 1x1 for a row that recorded nothing). (3) About half of xheavy's blank at 24k (8-11% before, Views 1.3%) was the frontier: one exact-thread turn of refine, paint and pictures ran 13-35 ms (32-38 turns over 12 ms in each 2 s), no stream left meanwhile, and the view ran past its last drawn row. A fast feed's pass now builds what its measured cost per row fits in 8 ms, the rest after the queued steps (the reader re-posts a pending pass): 4.0-5.6%. (4) The other half was WebView cards: the reader made a WebView for each and destroyed it with its row, white until its page painted, where RecyclerView hands Views a recycled one still showing its last page. The reader now keeps three released WebViews (paused) for the next rows: 0.8-1.6% at 24k, 0.1% at 12k, recorded fps 110 -> 117-118 (two runs; 6.5% in the same build with `webpool=0`). Measured and not kept: a pool of threads making a stream's pictures while the stream waits for them (4.8 ms of a long turn is `HardwareBuffer.create` and the copy): no change. Left: the long turns' paint (8.7 ms) and pictures (4.8 ms) still stall the stream (pictures made where they are decoded, or drawn into their slots when they land, would take the second out); crypto and heavy with the 8 ms pass are unmeasured (crypto's rows cost under 1 ms, so its passes should stay whole). Cold start on this phone (warm shader cache, five launches each, `results/video/xbstart-base`): content visible 216-227 ms against Views 311-322 and Compose 313-318, settled 306-313 against 453-472 and 472-476; both follow the system's launch transition, which starts at the app's first frame (73-84 ms, still blank; content is in the next, 88-100 ms, before the fade shows anything), so nothing in exact2's boot (19-22 ms of CPU) moves them here. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (`results/video/vwc`) the starting window stays until content, exact2's content frame (207-232 ms) is level with Views' (227-230), and that is where a shorter boot would show.
- **Android: crypto's 24k blank fixed; what the extra paints cost and what is left** (2026-10-05, android/cblank on android/explore 829cac9d1; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/cblank-*`). Cause (Perfetto, the reader's `cover=1`): a refine (every third scroll step, ~7 ms) mounts the rows a viewport past the view; they are out of view, so the pass is quiet and frames move the last paint until the next one, which came 0-4 frames later (median 17.6 ms after the refine); at 200 dp a frame the nearest of those rows is under two frames of travel away, so the list ended at the last drawn row for 2-4 frames of every five. The exact thread was not short of time (440 ms/s, the same before and after), and the main thread took the paint within 0.6 ms (median). Fix (`host/linux/src/canvas/travel.rs`): the host measures the feed's travel per step and since the last pass, and after a quiet pass paints by the frame its rows can first show (at once at 24k; ≤6k never sooner than before). Three runs each, interleaved, 12k/24k blank: before 0.0/19.8-24.2%, after 0.0/0.0%, Views 0.0/0.0%. Traced fling (two runs each): fps and janky frames unchanged at every speed; CPU and main ms/s unchanged to 12k; at 24k CPU 1,370 -> 1,455 ms/s and main 230 -> 253: the RenderThread now draws the fifth of the view that was blank (its drawing as at 12k), and paints go 25 -> ~47/s, each ingest costing the main thread ~0.9 ms whatever it carries (Views at 24k: 862 CPU, 341 main). xheavy (paints every frame, so unaffected): 24k blank 0.5-11.3% before, 8.3-10.7% after, five runs each; fps and CPU unchanged. Left: (1) the reader's per-stream ingest cost (or rows published without a paint: exact sends a new row's recording and place, the reader adds it to the feed's group) would take back the 24k main time. (2) `cover=1`'s counters read 0 while a fifth of the view was blank: not row reuse but the spacer row's bounds; fixed on android/xblank (the entry above). (3) A shifted window (`lead()` moved toward travel instead of grown) and a pass every step (`pre=1`, reader patch `~/bench/android/results/cblank-reader-pre.patch`) each left 2.8-3.2% at 24k; not kept. (4) The once-measured 0.3% crypto baseline was `blank.py`'s onset taking the launch animation for the driver's start (the entry above).
- **Android: blank areas while flinging fast — measured, the reader's share fixed, the rest owed** (2026-10-05, android/blank on 268878a98; Pixel 10 Pro XL; the reader is `~/bench/lists/exact/android/canvas`, outside the repo, patch `~/bench/android/results/blank/reader-blank.patch`). The measure: `~/bench/android/bin/bench.py blank <app>` records a fling with screenrecord and `bin/blank.py` takes, per driver segment, the share of the list's viewport in full-width one-colour bands ≥56 dp (no row there), time-weighted, the same arithmetic for every stack. Before (two runs each, 12k/24k dp/s): xheavy 2.5–3.4% / 23.1–23.4% (16–18% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 2.7–3.8%, crypto 0.3–0.5% / 24.0–24.2%; Views xheavy 1.6% / 1.0%, Compose 0.2% / 0.1%, Views heavy and crypto 0.0% (no frame more than half blank in either). The mechanism on xheavy: rows come into view as they are first recorded (median 2 ms after the stream that defines them), and the reader recorded every row not in view at paint time on its worker, so its group drew nothing there for a frame or more. With such rows recorded on main when within 33 ms of travel of the view, and a worker-made row's group recorded again before the next frame: xheavy 0.6–0.8% / 7.0–9.3% (2–5% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 0.0–0.1%; fps, late frames, main-thread ms/s and PSS unchanged, CPU within run spread. Owed: (1) crypto at 24k: landed, android/cblank (the entry above). (2) xheavy's rest at 24k: WebView cards not drawn yet (an empty bordered box, as Views shows) and a few whole-view blank frames. (3) Measured and not kept: a velocity lead (`refine(-1, v)`; the reader passed 0) made every report dearer and blanked more (xheavy 24k 18–19% vs 12–14% without, CPU +4–12%; a lead following the measured speed mounted and retired a row every report at 1k); keeping the velocity for passes between refines (`refine_slice` resets it to 0) tripled first recordings; moving the scrolled list to the front of the report queue (each pass takes two lists and every report queues every mounted list, inner ones too) measured no change; clamping the compositor's scroll to the drawn rows (as a browser clamps async scroll) left the blank (6.5–7.7%) and moved 98–99% of the distance. (4) The feed's group keeps rows at the list's start and end mounted (the reader saw rows millions of px away in every paint): memory and walk cost, not blank.
- **Image spools: work relative to the root's descriptor** (2026-10-06, the raster-spool review, Grok r3). `RasterSpool.establish` checks `tmp/exact-raster` is a real directory before and after taking `.namespace.lock`, then sweeps and makes its directory by path; another process of the same user swapping the root for a symlink between those steps could redirect them. Open the root `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` and use `openat`/`mkdirat`/`unlinkat` under it.
- **`rem`/`em` the JS target still bakes, or the browser resolves elsewhere** (2026-10-06, after #136): SVG geometry written as attributes (`svg_props`: `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `cx`, `cy`, `r` on viewports, uses, masks, patterns, filters and gradients) still carries the kernel's 16 px resolution; and a node whose element keeps the UA's font (`button[data-button-style]`'s `font: revert`, `select`, both ~13.33 px in Chrome) resolves an `em` width or padding against that size in the browser where the kernel uses the inherited one, so the JS target and the wasm page disagree there.
- **Linux draws a declared variable face at the requested weight** (2026-10-06, the review of the Apple variable-face fix). `snap_weight` (`host/linux/src/text/catalog.rs`) shapes a face declared at 400 at 600 when its file's `wght` axis covers it; the web (`@font-face` with one `font-weight`) and Apple pin each face to its declared weight. Make Linux pin it too, with a fixture test.
- **A computed style value that mixes a number and a string is checked by neither** (2026-10-06, the review of #137's fix). A string-typed binding on a row with no text form is refused (`takes_text`, `check_style_value`), but `opacity=(on ? 0.5 : label)` unifies to `Ty::Unknown` (`contract/lower/src/expr.rs`, `unify`) and compiles: macOS unsets the string arm, the browser applies it. Checking each arm's own type in `check_style_value`'s walk would close it.
- **The terminal surface's next work** (2026-10-06, LLP 1101.001 §3/§7; the terminal is admitted): P7's whitespace lint (`lint-space-collapses`) and the pitfalls' box-model candidate; P12 kitty images in the live region by id; `scripts/agent.mjs terminal` over the headless verbs and a `record` verb; the `color` fact and quantization below truecolour (D5); the profile's admission holes (the `border` shorthand, `style` blocks, non-literal lengths; both 1101.000 reviews); a non-terminal entry refusing `ch`/`lh` until a GUI host resolves font-relative units (Astra); ibex2's lossless stream mode when a consumer streams unreproducible events through the executor (P4); LLP 1101.002's deferred P5 (retirement's view half in the runner, now that `printed` is a contract) and its Q2 (start-aligned buttons, `nowrap` rows in the terminal sheet).
- **LLP 1075.003.000.001, access hatches on every platform** (2026-10-06, accepted as a design; landing on main as it is built; its §13 is the as-built record). Done: the rename from hooks; of stage 1, on the web's JS target and on Apple, every call timed, `diagnostics` with its bounds, `state.hatches`' new shape, `perf hatches`, Save Trace's section and the web's throw rule. Also done: the per-platform `hatches` declaration with its typed keys, the plan's requirement, the cohort's capability, `classifyArtifacts`' branch, hosts that call only the words their module handles (`platform`, `unhandled`), late frames that name their hatches, the crash breadcrumb and `EXACT_HATCHES=off` on Apple. The plan's own per-platform row is built for Apple (a `hatches` table; a word not given to the platform reaches Swift unhatched). A node's calls are timed by its plan site on every host. Owed of stage 1: the wall-clock acceptance driven in a person's session (a late frame from a 30 ms hatch, read from Save Trace), and §9's cost series on a phone. Of stage 2, done on the web and Apple: the bounded act queue, `input(text)`, settle draining and naming a hatch that never stops, the app and window scopes with the embedder's two grants, the frame clock and `after` with the seek stopping at each hatch instant, and `?hatches=off` on the web. Root `data-*` words reach `app` as `app.data` on every host. Owed of stage 2: `input`'s remaining cases (a search controller keeping first responder, composition). Then: stage 3's leftovers (a part tap driven as a real touch on the simulator, and whether `owns` should take a `UIInteraction`, whose recognizers a hatch cannot declare today); Stage 4 is built on Linux (the trait, the overlay, observed input, the scopes, the frame clock, diagnostics, the breadcrumb); it builds, lints and passes the fixture smoke on a real Linux box too (x86_64, 2026-10-07); its Windows adapter is written and has never been compiled for Windows or run (a `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` from Linux stops at `ring`'s C build, so it needs a Windows machine or a Windows C toolchain, and a Windows crate for the fixture), the Linux `build.rs` of each Rust-data app includes its hatches (`rust_hatch_entry`), the six that bake through `exact_js_bake` (canvas-gallery, completion-storm, duo-lab, expose, messages, weatherlight) do not yet, and an overlay draws no images or text yet. Stage 5, the `hatch` verb, the template's section and the guides, is done. Stage 6: Android runs stage 4's hatches (its in-repo host is the Linux presenter built for Android; 38 of 38 on a Pixel 10 Pro XL and a Pixel 10a); a Kotlin module and a `view` for a node that is a View wait for the Canvas reader to be in this repository, with their own RFC, and `smoke.mjs` has no `android` host. The design's r4 had no review before the ruling, so review should look first at the mid-seek act drain, each host's `input` primitive and the plan's per-platform words.
- **`cargo test -p exact-linux --lib` and `--test pinned` segfault on a Linux box with a real GPU** (2026-10-07, minisforum: x86_64, Radeon 890M, Mesa Vulkan). The crash is in `libvulkan.so.1` on a `Vello shader init` thread while dozens of test threads each start a GPU painter; it is there at aece7f3a7 too, before the Linux hatches. `-- --test-threads=1` passes all of both (623 and 76). The async lane's builders have no GPU, so nothing reports it. Fix to measure: one Vulkan instance a process for the tests, or a lock around instance creation.
- **`agent macos` is not under App Transport Security** (2026-10-06, #106): the driver runs the bare `standalone/ExactMac`, which has no bundle `Info.plist`, so an `iframe` of `http://<named host>` loads in a drive and shows the ATS error in the `.app` unless `host.macos.appTransportSecurity` allows it; so does a named-host `http:` sub-resource of an app's own page (#135). `EXACT_MAC_BIN=<.app>/Contents/MacOS/ExactMac` drives the bundle and shows what a user sees, but skips the freshness check. Fix: drive the assembled bundle's executable by default, or link the plist into the bare executable (`-sectcreate __TEXT __info_plist`, unmeasured: whether ATS reads an embedded plist).
- **Four `scripts/app.test.mjs` deploy-capture tests fail since f0a074f32** (2026-10-06): "deploy excludes generated shells…", "R12 deploy captures initialized dependency submodules", "R13 capture refuses tracked files…" and "R14 external game capture…" run `snapshotOf` in the shell fixture's workspace, and `deploy.mjs`'s `contractSources` (LLP 1091 stage 2) runs `cargo run -p contract` there: "package(s) `contract` not found in workspace". The fixture needs the compiler from the SDK's workspace, or `contractSources` needs to run it from there. The file is not in the gate, so nothing reported it.
- **iOS: `share` under an app-owned tab container** (2026-10-07; the Brooks port's bar Share did nothing in its fork). Exact's own containers are fine: a native-fixture build with no agent pushed Detail, then shared from a timer (no source node, so `shareAnchor` falls back to the window's root view and the root controller presents, the path Brooks' hidden bar-item source takes), and the system sheet came up over the pushed route. Brooks uses its own tab container (`BrooksTabs`, through the `tabContainer` hatch, LLP 1075.003 §3.6); that is the case left to try, and the fork's `presenter(from:)` (present from the visible controller) is the fix to measure against it.
- **PhotosUI and AuthenticationServices add ~1.1 s each to an iOS simulator launch** (2026-10-05, Signal clone feel pass). exec→main is ~1.3 s for an exact2 app on the iOS 27 simulator, ~185 ms for a bare UIKit app; linking either framework alone into the bare app brings it to ~1.3–1.4 s (dyld_sim parsing Mach-O headers), every other framework the host links costs under 30 ms. macOS's dyld shows no measurable cost for the same two, so a phone very likely pays nothing: this is the simulator's (agent drives, smoke tests, anyone judging feel there). Fix: `Picker.swift` (PHPicker) and `Auth.swift` (ASWebAuthenticationSession) behind an artifact loaded on first use, as the GPU, video and web modules are (a new artifact: needs a human's yes).
- **Live Snapback reads without a timer** (2026-10-08, [LLP 1110](llp/1110-live-snapback-reads.rfc.md), Draft): every app in the app farm's client arm refreshed on a 2–15 s Contract task, because a held `poll(20)` holds `clock settle`. Snapback serves `/changes` as SSE (its lead session), `Snapback.changes` in `snapback4/ts` opens it as a stream resource, reading resources take its `seq` as an argument. Needs review, then the Snapback endpoint.
- **Snapback's generated API types cannot be imported into a captured app** (2026-10-08, app farm, nest-architects-0337): `snapback/generated/api.ts` imports `snapback4`'s declarations, and the bake refuses `module outside captured app: …/node_modules/snapback4/dist/receipt-family.d.ts`, so builders retype their rows by hand. Either the bake admits type-only imports from `node_modules` (no runtime bytes), or `snapback4 types` writes a self-contained file; decide which side owns it.
- **A stream that can send: Supabase Realtime and Convex without polling** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: an equipment-lending app built on Snapback4, Convex and Supabase polled on all three). `fetch(url, { exactStream })` reads SSE and a receive-only WebSocket (`docs/reference.md`, "An answer that keeps coming"), so a feed that waits for a subscribe frame (Phoenix's join, Convex's sync protocol) cannot be read. An RFC: frames sent on open, then app-to-socket sends tied to the resource's arguments, under `net.websocket`, on Hermes, the wasm web host and the JS target alike; Supabase and Convex as the proof.
- **npm in `app.ts`, and backend SDKs** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: three handwritten wire adapters, Supabase's session persistence and token refresh by hand). Two problems. Bundling: the bake refuses outside imports "until dependency capture is implemented" (`js/bake/src/lib.rs`, `sources`), and LLP 1091 D10's package linking for Contract is most of that capture. The model: an SDK lives between answers (refresh timers, a socket it writes, `localStorage`), and a data module runs only inside one, refusing a `fetch` or storage call outside it. Pure packages work once bundling does; an SDK needs a client lifetime beyond one answer or a first-party adapter. An RFC with supabase-js and the Convex client as the cases; offline (snapshot cache, purge on sign-out, the persisted half of the keep-last-value line below) follows from it.
- **CSS `visibility: hidden` on Apple, what is left** (2026-10-05, after e28279b3b, 3703cc461 and 2298d07e3; a held focus, tvOS focus, hidden-text targets and the agent's `layout` followed): a hidden box's `backgroundMaterial` view and Mac backdrop blur still show (gating `materialRequest` alone tears the effect down and reparents its children, which drops a visible descendant's focus on iOS, and lets a glass group form, `glassGroupConflict`); hiding a Mac field or text area that is being edited (`applyCssVisibility` sets its `isHidden`) ends the edit at once, and its `change` (`commitEdit` calls `onChange` directly) applies a batch inside the style batch; a Mac text selection keeps a hidden paragraph's text (filtering it in `TextSelection.selectable` would shift a virtual list's paragraph ordinals from the runner's projection); iOS background-colour keyframes on the layer (`SvgScene`) still paint; a visible inline link reads its whole range to VoiceOver (`InlineAccessibility`), hidden nested spans included.
- **An update store reopened while another thread spawns is refused** (2026-10-05, review of the exact-update test fix): a spawned child holds a copy of `owner.lock`'s descriptor until its exec closes it, so `Store::open` (d3c84c2ba) on one thread while another is inside `Command::spawn` finds an "exclusive owner". The hosts open the store once at launch and hold it, so nothing in the tree does this; an embedder that reopens would need to serialize the two, as the test harness does (`open_store`, `finished`).
- **Transitions and animations of SVG `points` and `d`** (2026-10-05, the Signal clone): Chrome interpolates a polygon's `points` and a path's `d` (`path()`) between shapes with matching commands, which makes Lottie-style morphs declarative; Contract has no such transition or keyframe value, so a morph is a canvas or a stack of crossfades.
- **Shared ExactKit test classes never run on iOS** (2026-10-05, the Brooks port's feedback). `build.mjs --test --ios` runs only classes named `*IOSTests`, so a shared class with `#if os(iOS)` branches, such as `SurfaceControlTests`, is compiled for iOS and never run there: its `testContactIdentityIncludesCanvasAndReleaseRequest` failed on iOS until the macOS `!isSurfaceControl` blur guard was copied over, and no gate saw it. Run the shared classes on iOS too, fixing or marking what fails there. On a1ead713c, an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) also failed nine assertions in `GroupedListIOSTests` (expects a 300×600 window, gets 402×874), `NavigationCollapseIOSTests` and `NavigationTabsIOSTests`, which an iPhone Duo (iOS 27) passed: the suite should pin its simulator or stop depending on it.
- **LLP 1099's unmeasured inputs** (2026-10-05, [LLP 1099](llp/1099-uikit-springs.rfc.md) §4.6, stage 0): a device recording of one duration-form and one negative-bounce spring (tracked with the Signal clone's `motion-trace.mjs`); older iOS runtimes, with availability guards in `motion/tests/fixtures/uikit-springs/probe/`; mixed-form retargets; a dense duration-form sweep below u = 0.05 at ζ ≤ 0.1. None blocks stages 1–3; each would amend the fixtures.
- **A todo app's first native build compiles wgpu, vello, the game engine and canvas** (2026-10-04, the LLP 1086 fresh-agent trial). A fresh `exact new` app's first macOS and iOS builds took ~13 minutes each, building crates the app never uses ("GPU: no GPU crate" in the same log). The README now says ten to fifteen minutes; pay-for-what-you-use (LLP 1047) says they should not be built at all for such an app.
- **Journal a route whose overflow nothing scrolls** (2026-10-05, Exact-new iOS feedback; `lower-route-scroll` covers the static half). A runner log was built and pulled after GPT-6-Astra and Grok 4.7 reviews: it read every layout offer as the route's box, so a sheet detent or `resizes-content` keyboard flagged a covered screen and latched its key; covered retained routes, absolute and transformed descendants, and host covers (padding, which Taffy's content bottom omits) gave wrong answers; and it ran on the boot layout. A real one journals only the active top route, at the settled window offer, after first pixel, counting in-flow content against the padding edge, and clears its key when the route fits again.
- **Authoring-bench round 3 leftovers** (2026-10-05, LLP 1087, Claude and Codex builders on 15fae50b4/1811a9c98):
  `text-decoration-line: line-through` on a `textarea` draws on the web, not on iOS; a test cannot assert list order (`expect state doing.0.id` is a syntax error; builders join ids in a derive); a `pan`/`panrelease` payload has no pointer position, so a drop target is computed from frame + offset + deltas + a hard-coded inset; `outline` is not admitted (inset `box-shadow` instead); a JS drive script kept in the app folder makes the web build stale on every edit to it.
- **Authoring-bench iOS round 6 leftovers** (2026-10-05, LLP 1087, web+ios on b6b3332a4): on iOS a SQLite-backed mutation lands a turn after the input, so a tap at the new row misses, and the miss names no in-flight request there (the hint is web-only because a native read on the error path could time out and close the transport, 3c27a248c), where a failed `expect` with no input before it does name them.
- **`max-width`/`max-height` take `auto` and refuse `none`** (2026-10-05, authoring bench r30 t4): CSS's initial maximum is `none` and `auto` is not a CSS maximum, so `max-height=(narrow ? "320px" : "none")` is refused while a non-CSS spelling passes (the web is the standard). The refusal now says to leave the attribute out or write `auto`; admitting `none` (as Taffy's unbounded max) is a compiler change.
- **`tree --ax` `pressed` on native hosts** (2026-10-05, authoring bench): the Chrome path keeps an aria-pressed toggle's `pressed` state; UIKit (`toggleButton`, "0"/"1"/"2") and AppKit (`AXCheckBox`/`AXToggle`, which emits `checked` and drops mixed) do not, and `CAN`/`APPLIES` in `scripts/agent-ax.mjs` do not compare it.
- **Two tabs of one web app: `sqlite.open` refuses with EBUSY** (2026-10-05, authoring bench audit a2-contacts): the second tab's data module gets EBUSY rather than waiting for the lock, and a source has no timer to back off with; either `open` waits (bounded) or the docs say one tab holds the database.
- **Authoring-bench iOS round 13 polish** (2026-10-05, web+ios on cdfbd9e1e): a `button appearance="auto"` is tiny on the web until given a width and height (iOS sizes it); a `role="tablist"`'s authored background draws as a frame around the native segmented control on iOS; on iOS an `input type="date"` with an explicit width centres its control in the box (`text-align` has no effect; a `select` honours it since #253), and without one a select's empty-option title clips off the left; an empty date input shows today's date on iOS (UIDatePicker cannot be blank) where the web shows an empty field; under the agent a pushed screen's native bar is not presented, so a builder could not see it in screenshots.
- **A `role="tablist"` has no default look on the web** (2026-10-05, authoring bench iOS round 15, t1-tip): on iOS it is a `UISegmentedControl`; on the web its tabs are bare run-on labels ("15%18%20%25%") until styled, and the styling's track then shows as a rim around the native control on iOS. A web default for a segmented tablist, or a way to style only the web's, would let one view serve both.
- **A test's `reload` restarts at the start URL** (2026-10-05, authoring bench round 5, t3-recipes: ~10 min): it keeps the store but boots at the drive's launch URL, not the route the test reached, so "reload on a detail screen shows that recipe" cannot be an authored test; a browser's reload keeps the URL. Either `reload` keeps the current location (as a person's reload does) or the docs say it does not.
- **`includes` is text-only; a list has no membership test** (2026-10-05, authoring bench, LLP 1087: a Codex builder on t6-todo-more wrote `includes(t.tags, tag)` and got "argument 1 expects string, given list<string>", then counted a filter). The web's `Array.prototype.includes` takes a list; and there is still no text→number parse (`toLowerCase` and `formatNumber` have landed).
- **Lengths and numbers where HTML and CSS take both** (2026-10-04/05, authoring bench, LLP 1087: a Codex builder and three Claude builders). `min`, `max` and `step` on `input type="number"` take strings while a range input takes numbers (`lower-attr-type`); `letter-spacing="1px"` is refused (`expected number`), though CSS's `letter-spacing` is a length. Each message says the fix, but the web accepts all three as written.
- **`visibility: visible` under a hidden ancestor, natively** (2026-10-04, b6 review B3; LLP 1001's declared deviation). CSS paints a descendant that sets its own `visible`; Apple hides the subtree with `isHidden`, and the Linux painter draws the hidden box's whole content through `Unpainted` (`paint.rs`, `drawn`). Linux could restore its backend before the children and skip only the box's own paint; Apple needs a view's own drawing (fills, borders, text, controls) hidden apart from its subviews, which `isHidden` and layer opacity cannot say. No app has needed it yet.
- **`--chrome platform` with `--touch platform`: a finger cannot aim at a bar item** (2026-10-04, fix/ios7). With UIKit's bars shown, a tap naming a tab or a header control presses it by host activation; under real touches it is refused ("drawn by … tap it without --touch platform"), since the node is not where UIKit draws its item. Aiming at the item's own frame (`UITabBar`'s, the bar button's view) would make it a real touch. Also: `screenshot … window` draws the window, not the software keyboard's window; the tab bar's selection animates on real time (`clock +300 real`).
- **A text field's caret moves are no event** (2026-10-04, fix/input7, codeedit #2). `input` and `change` carry the selection and `select` fires on a person's non-empty selection or a `setSelectionRange`, as HTML's; a plain caret move (a click, an arrow) is only the web's element-level `selectionchange`, which Contract admits on `text` alone. Admitting `selectionchange` on `input` and `textarea`, with the `InputEvent`, would let an editor follow its caret without a key handler; every host keeps the selection now.
- **Linux fields have no text hit-testing** (2026-10-04, fix/input7). A click in a field puts the caret at its end, and a textarea's caret and selection place a soft-wrapped line wrong (positions count hard breaks only); Up/Down in a textarea and word moves are not implemented.
- **Linux pointer phases and drags differ from the page's** (2026-10-04, batch 6: LLP 1094 D12 made the conformance's Linux reference take `down`, `move`, `up` and `drag`, which showed these; `LINUX_APART` stops each comparison at its first one). A mouse press does not focus the pressed button on Linux, as the page does (synthetic-press, synthetic-rowsmore); a drag past a list's top does not autoscroll, so `synthetic-reorder`'s `grip-5` lands before photo-00015, not photo-00000; interaction-gallery's sheet height drag ends at 521 against the page's 518; textflow's orb drag advances `elapsed` (288) where the page's stays 0. Each is the Linux host's; fix it and drop its `LINUX_APART` line.
- **An empty `text` is a line tall natively, zero on the web** (2026-10-04, found driving batch 6's scratch app). `text note` with `note = ""` lays out 18 px tall on macOS (`layout note`: h 18) and 0 on the web (a `<div>` with no content has no line box), so a status line that starts empty pushes what follows down a line on Apple only. Batch 6 touched no kernel text or layout code; the kernel's measure of an empty run is the likeliest place. The web is the standard: an empty text should have no line.
- **The Lean `stdlib`'s equations are near their heartbeat budget** (2026-10-04, impl/1088-lists, LLP 1088 §9.1). Lean realizes `Contract.stdlib`'s equation lemmas where a proof first unfolds it, under the command line's options (a `set_option` does not reach them), and its match over the roster's string names took about 200,000 heartbeats before `concat` and needs more after, so `semantics/lakefile.toml` raises `maxHeartbeats` for the library. Each roster entry grows it (origin/main's formats and `t` add more). Matching the router's thirteen entries in a function of their own, reached from one arm, would shrink the match; `stdlib_ty`'s and `stdlib_good`'s case analysis would follow.
- **Lists: `find`** (2026-10-04, impl/1088-lists). `indexOf` and `split` landed (LLP 1088 §9.1, the 2026-10-04 note); `find` (a record by a field, the open row itself rather than its position) would take `indexOf`'s budget term, one step an item scanned, but its callback makes it a `map`/`filter`-style opcode, not a roster call. Its refusal says to compute it in the data source meanwhile.
- **`perf frames live` on Apple** (2026-10-04, fix/game, the platformer's diary R11). The web's wasm target lends the agent's clock to the wall for a window (LLP 1079 D4); macOS and iOS answer `virtual`. `FrameSampler.swift` already builds the reply; a live branch in `Agent.swift`'s `perf` would release `session.clock` for the window, turn the GPU module's seek off (`GpuModule` takes `agentFreezes`) and let the display link fire runner timers (`Session.swift` gates them on `!agentMode`), then take the clock back at the wall.

- **`z-index` across a non-stacking parent, as CSS** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, chess #5, kanban, kanban2, LLP 1087 t4-kanban). LLP 1083.000 §3.1 holds every host to "`z-index` stays inside its holder" because Apple nests one view per node and `zPosition` orders only one layer's sublayers. The web would follow CSS by dropping isolation rule (z) (rules a and b keep native tree order for level 0); the Linux painter could defer an escaping box to its stacking context's phase with its ancestors' clip and transform (hit testing follows its box order); Apple would have to re-parent an escaping view to the context's view with the intermediate clips and transforms rebuilt, as flights and the Arrange lift do for one view. Deciding between one order everywhere and CSS where a host can is Charlie's; the pitfall and LLP 1001 say what holds now.
- **Native `frame()` reads transforms as committed** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, LLP 1051.000 D1). A transform in flight, a 3D one's projection and a `-exact-layout-transition`'s offset are the web's alone (its client rect); natively the runner composes the rows. Hosts noting presented transforms as they note scroll offsets (`Runner::scrolled`) would close it, for the first consumer that reads geometry mid-motion.
- **`text-align="center"` on an `input` is ignored on iOS** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, LLP 1087: the tip and pomodoro apps on the iOS 27 simulator, two trials). It centers on the web; on iOS the text sits at the left. Check `NodeViewIOS`'s text field alignment.
- **Authored tests cannot press the browser's back button** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, t3-recipes on web+iOS). `tap <id> history -1` is a CLI drive op only, so "the browser back returns to the list" is checked by hand; a test-file step (web only, refused elsewhere) would let the test say it.
- **An action cannot start a timer** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, t6-todo-more: all three builders). A 5-second undo offer needed a root `every(50, tick)` comparing `now()` with a stored deadline, so expiry is quantized to the tick; builders asked for a task an action starts (or `after` as an action command). A language question for Charlie.
- **Form attributes the web has and Contract refuses** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, rounds 1–2): `autocomplete` on `input`, and `label`/`for` (builders used `aria-label` plus a visible `text`). (`aria-invalid` and CSS-wide `inherit`, also met there, landed the same day.)
- **A Lean theorem for LLP 1089 D3** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1089](llp/1089-action-composition.rfc.md) §7.2). If no path reads a slot another frame assigned earlier on that path, loading each slot as the last assignment on the path (or its starting value) equals the snapshot load; derives, resources, `pending` and sends lie outside the statement. That would prove `analyze-call-stale-read` sound against `exec`. No implementer yet.
- **Rewrite the diaries' copies as calls** (2026-10-04, LLP 1089 §4). Files' four opens as `arrive(path)` (F27, which also fixes `openFocused`'s missing `anchorPath = ""`), and spreadsheet's close-the-editor, `followSelection` and `clipKey` → `move` (F21), each driven on web and macOS. A scratch copy of spreadsheet with `commitEdit()` passes its web tests.
- **A scrolling `fit-content` route measures its rows as laid out in the sheet** (2026-10-07, Astra's review of `bsky/content-detent`, LLP 1075.003 §9.11). Its detent reads the scroll extent, so rows that may shrink (`flex-shrink: 1`) hold the sheet short; the non-scrolling route is measured alone (`Kernel::fit_content_height`). A second extent on the `content` op for the detent would end the `flex-shrink: 0` advice.
- **tvOS has no test target** (2026-10-04, fix/impl1088, LLP 1088 D7.3). `canBecomeFocused` is false for an explicit negative `tabindex` (`RemoteTVOS.swift`), and it compiles (`bun host/apple/build.mjs --tvos`), but nothing runs it: `KeyboardFocusIOSTests` is `#if os(iOS)` and `build.mjs --test` has no `--tvos`. The D7.3 test list's tvOS line waits on one.
- **A `pan` area takes a mouse drag that starts on selectable text** (2026-10-04, fix/typo, the reader diary). The reader's paged mode pans its page area by pointer, so a mouse drag over a paragraph with `selectionchange` turns the page instead of selecting (web: `input-glue.js` `pan` prevents the pointerdown; scroll mode selects on web and macOS). A rule like rule 3's for controls — a mouse contact that starts on a `text` with `selectionchange` is the selection's, a touch still pans — is a gesture-arbitration decision (LLP 1057).
- **A fragmented paragraph is one view on Apple, not one layer per fragment** (2026-10-05, LLP 1093 D8, left when the reader port landed). `Columns.swift` draws the paragraph clipped per fragment inside its union-sized view, so a paragraph across a spread's gutter backs a layer two pages wide. Hits already refuse the gap, and the reader's page clips, so nothing in the port showed it.
- **Content regions take no `text-indent`, `hyphens: auto` or a soft hyphen's fit** (2026-10-04, fix/typo). `host/apple/src/content_region/wire.rs` sends neither row, and `RegionWorkerLayout` breaks without the indent and without the hyphen-fit rule `TextEngine.layout` has; justification and soft-hyphen ink are there. Thread both through the region wire when a region consumer wants book text.
- **The document window, what the studio pass left** (2026-10-04, fix/macdocs; LLP 1069.010 "As built: the document window"). A ⌘Q that a window's `beforeunload` keeps open is cancelled, not resumed once the app's question is answered (NSDocument's review continues the quit); `wheel` and `drop` have no iPadOS form (a trackpad's scroll, a drag from Files) and `title` no `UIToolTipInteraction`; a command's menu is by chord only, so Zoom to Fit ⌘9 stays in File beside View's zooms; the bake does not warn when a declared extension already belongs to another app's type on the Mac (Freeform's `.board`); repeating gradients and length stops are refused everywhere rather than painted natively (the kernel takes percentage stops only).
- **A resource whose arguments change reads its shape's zero until the new reply** (2026-10-04, fix/tooling3, workout F6). Lift's diary reports its board resource's `ready: false` unmounting the whole screen on every search or filter change, a flash on real time and a zero board under a test's still clock; the web's data stacks (SWR, React Query's `keepPreviousData`) keep the last value while a refetch is in flight. Native `clock settle` already waits for every runner request (a parked storage turn included; the macOS drive could not reproduce F6's miss on this branch), so what is left is the semantics: keep the last answer, flagged pending, until the new one lands, on every host.
- **The JS target's bundle warns of exact's own glue** (2026-10-04, fix/tooling3): `ts-data.js` imports `kept` from `app.ts`, which most apps don't export, so every TypeScript app's web build logs `IMPORT_IS_UNDEFINED` twice. `host/web/build.mjs` now prints only the compiler's `warning:` lines; read the export optionally (a namespace import) so the bundler has nothing to say.
- **iOS sound is silenced by the ring/silent switch** (2026-10-04, fix/audio, LLP 1042 §8). An `audio` or `video` plays under the default AVAudioSession category, which the switch mutes; Safari's media element plays through it. The app-scoped audio-session arbiter of LLP 1042 §5 would pick `.playback` for media with sound.
- **A native data module's `console` never reaches the agent's `logs`** (2026-10-04, fix/audio, trivia F7). Hermes collects each line (`js/src/shim.cc` `install_console`, `Module::take_logs`), and `js/src/lib.rs`'s header says they reach the runner's journal, but no host calls `take_logs` and `DataSource` has no hook for it, so a module's `console.error` (the storage-outside-an-answer warning included) is lost on macOS, iOS and Linux, where the web's page console reaches `logs`. A `DataSource` method the runner drains after each answer and fulfil, forwarded through `Storage`, `Mixed` and `Placed`, would carry them.
- **Hear LLP 1096 on a phone** (2026-10-04, impl/1096). The sound arm's interruption handling (a call, Siri: the engine stops, voices due meanwhile are journaled `sound skipped: interrupted`, the engine restarts) and `audio_session: "playback"` against the ring/silent switch were built and run on the iOS simulator only; drums on an iPhone, and tvOS's tier-2 build, are still to do.
- **`z-index="auto"` is refused** (2026-10-04, files F14, the lists lane). `z_index` is an `i32` row defaulting to 0, so a conditional `z-index` cannot fall back to CSS's `auto`, and `auto` and `0` differ in CSS (only `0` makes a stacking context). A lifted, dragged row writes `z-index=(lifted ? 2 : 0)` for now.
- **Caltrain's web test fails on headless Linux Chrome** (2026-10-04, the authoring bench's Hetzner boxes, main at 469f5ada8 and 7a9825e91): `bun scripts/agent.mjs web --test apps/caltrain/app.test.contract` → "changing the station goes through the stations screen: line 12: tap #17 at (229.5, 85.5): div covers its middle", every run, with Playwright's Chromium 1234 and DejaVu fonts. Look at what covers `change-station` there (a font-dependent layout, or a sheet still presenting).
- **Should `clock settle` fire a pending one-shot timer?** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, LLP 1087 t7-wizard.) A `task boot mount` with `after(1, restore)` does not fire at `clock settle` alone (it waits for requests and advances to the end of motion, LLP 1012 §2, firing a timer only if it reaches it on the way); a builder needed `clock +50`. The docs said a timer lands at `clock settle` and now say what it does. Including the earliest pending `after` (not `every`, which has no fixed point) in settle's target would match what builders expect; Charlie's call.
- **`exact-update`'s `publisher::the_published_stream_is_staged_whole_by_a_client` flakes under load** (2026-10-04, the authoring bench's Hetzner box at load ~20): about 1 run in 3, `update store /tmp/exact-publisher-<pid>-stream is unavailable (exclusive owner): lock acquisition failed because the operation would block` (`update/tests/it/publisher.rs:287`). Alone it passes.
- **Setup and the wasm build on a fresh Linux box** (2026-10-04, the authoring bench): With a private `CARGO_HOME` the `-Zbuild-std` path failed offline on std's own crates (`rustc-demangle`) until `cargo fetch --manifest-path <sysroot>/…/library/Cargo.toml` was run by hand, though `app.mjs` means to do that itself. `host/web/build.mjs` renames into `EXACT_WEB_DIST` without creating its parent (ENOENT).
- **The chooser, what is left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1021](llp/1021-menus.rfc.md) "The chooser", "The chooser on macOS", "Placement"). Four gaps remain. The native `UIMenu`/`NSMenu` presentations are covered only by XCTest: under the agent a menu paints (D4), and a real touch into one awaits LLP 1080.000 stage 3. The iOS sheet cannot checkmark the remembered row, because `UIAlertAction` has no public checked state. macOS still lists a menu's hide-only Cancel row as an item. `position-area` is unread by `UIMenu` and Linux.
- **A Mac canvas draws some of its native text upside down since cc60cf219** (2026-10-04; found driving Caltrain after a build-speed change, bisected on the M4 over 157 commits with a capture compared by pixels). Caltrain's header card (the name, "Change station", "Web deck", the theme row, the train), the search field's text and "Back" are each flipped top to bottom in place, on screen and in the agent's screenshot; plain text rows beside them ("Matches", "Palo Alto") are upright, and two dividers are gone. `cc60cf219` ("compose macOS canvas uploads in current paint order") is the first commit that shows it: `Capture.bitmap` stopped using `cacheDisplay` and renders a copied layer tree with `CALayer.render(in:)` (`paintOrderBitmap`), which does not draw an AppKit view's flipped contents the way AppKit does. `bun scripts/agent.mjs macos "tap change-station" "type station-search Palo" "clock settle" "screenshot out.png"` shows it. This belongs with the entry below: whichever capture is kept has to draw those views upright. It is also the macOS smoke's one failure on a 1x Mac: the sky fixture's "Zoom" and "Label" are upside down (1.58% of pixels beyond the band against `canvas-sky.macos.420x120.png`, which shows them upright). Ruled out (fix/health6, 2026-10-04, four tries reverted): `contentsAreFlipped()` does not tell the turned layers from the upright ones — Caltrain's header texts and its result rows are both `NSViewBackingLayer`s with `NSViewBackingLayerContents`, `contentsAreFlipped() == true` under the one geometry-flipped layer above the overlay's root, identity `contentsTransform`, the same gravity, rect and format, yet the copy draws the header turned and the rows upright. Turning every copied layer whose count of flipped ancestors differs fixes the header and turns the rows; redrawing every view before the copy changes nothing; holding the copy under a geometry-flipped layer mirrors the whole picture with the same split. What the window knows about each backing store that the copy loses is still to be found; the likeliest is an orientation `NSViewBackingLayerContents` carries from when it was drawn (the split differed between runs, the train image upright in one and turned in another), which the window server honours and `render(in:)` does not. The candidate fix is to give the copy each drawing view's own picture as a `CGImage` (its `draw(_:)` into a bitmap, as `cacheDisplay` did before cc60cf219) in place of its backing store; a single NSView fixture captured both ways would confirm it first.
- **Two ordered macOS captures after LLP 1083.000 met main's capture fix** (2026-10-04). The agent's screenshot uses `Capture.picture` (`AgentMac.swift`, 4863d26d7: subviews re-sorted by `zPosition` around `cacheDisplay`, presented animation values, box fills hidden); canvas uploads use LLP 1083.000's `paintOrderBitmap` (`Mac/CaptureMac.swift`: an explicit compositor, filter pictures, model values with animated key paths overlaid). Both order by the paint rank and both pass the paint-order driver; keep one, with both sets of tests (`PaintCaptureMacTests`, the shop/spreadsheet captures).
- **CSS font generic mappings for cursive/fantasy/math/emoji/fangsong and CSS-wide font-family values** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): currently refused precisely so a generic is never silently treated as a local family.
- **CSS intrinsic flex-basis keywords (content/min-content/max-content/fit-content)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): dimension rows need an intrinsic sizing representation; compiler now diagnoses the declared mode limit precisely.
- **CSS native interaction/painter gaps (diaries kanban F11, minesweeper F4, ledger F1, pomodoro F2)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): resize handles; iOS/Linux user-select text/all/contain; dotted/dashed and other border styles; decoration color/style/thickness and Linux underline skip-ink:auto. Current compiler/driver diagnoses each declared limit.
- **Linux authored glyph fallback cascades** (kanban F8, 2026-10-04): font lists select the first available family; cosmic-text still owns glyph fallback through its single Attrs.family. Preserve the rest of the authored list per shaping run before claiming full CSS per-glyph fallback parity.
- **Layout property transitions** (kanban F15, shop F5, 2026-10-04): CSS `transition="width 200ms ease"` is valid CSS, but the native engine cannot interpolate arbitrary layout rows or lay out per frame. The diagnostic names supported paint/transform/SVG properties and the admitted height path. Add width and other layout transitions only with an approved executor/layout design; `-exact-layout-transition` projects changes to the laid-out box today.
- **Viewport units on scalar CSS lengths** (kanban F9, 2026-10-04): dimension rows retain viewport units across wire and resolve on native resize; font-size, gap, spacing and border widths still use scalar rows. Extend their retained relative lengths and web emission before admitting viewport units there.
- **Native generic CSS visibility is not applied to paint/hit** (2026-10-04, found during LLP 1015.003). The shared Linux/Windows Presenter still paints and hits an ordinary box with `visibility:hidden`; its route/inert checks and SVG visibility are different paths. Qualify inherited visibility, explicit visible descendants and retained A/B scene ownership before correcting this separate style gap.

- **Clock timelines, what the reviews left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1055.002](llp/1055.002-synced-animations.rfc.md) §5 "Known gaps"). Under the web agent clock an author's pause and resume is recorded once (`held`, as on main for every animation), so a resumed synced animation does not rejoin there, and one JS-agent seek that crosses a timer registers its joiner before older finite members are sought. `contract symbols` has no definition or reference for a `timeline`. Not yet driven after the review fixes: Lexy (its repository) on iOS and the web, macOS and Linux apps, Firefox, Core Animation across backgrounding.
- **The web size gate, after 2026-10-06**: gated on the production app.js. Budgets: Caltrain 25 KiB (22.7 shipped), RealWorld 38 (36.0), the video player 17 (15.6), set by Charlie after lane/web-size's cuts. Further cuts: strip the development-only `pf()` wrappers from `--production` (lane/web-followups); RealWorld's runtime core and data layer.
- **`release` is every developer tool's profile too** (2026-10-04, the build-speed pass; LLP 1036.000 §5–7). The Linux host an agent drives and an app's render entry moved to `host-dev`, where a touched kernel line rebuilds in 3.6 s; at `release` (one codegen unit, thin LTO) it was 102 s on a Ryzen 3600, 58 of them the kernel on one core. So did the Contract compiler the smokes run for their fixtures. The web's tools are still `cargo run --release`: the dev loop's `exact-dev` and `exact-js-bake` (`host/web/dev.mjs`), `parity.mjs`, `logic/bake` (`scripts/rust.mjs`), the `metrics` bins, and conformance's wasm side. Each rebuilds through that link after any change under it. Moving them to `host-dev` is the same one-line change each, left for the web's pass.
- **What `release` could shed: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Hetzner Ryzen 3600, `caltrain-linux` / `caltrain-render`, stripped). Thin LTO, one codegen unit, `opt-level = 3` (today): 17.91 / 8.00 MB, 134 s from nothing. Fat LTO: 16.44 / 7.31 MB (−8%), 207 s. `opt-level = "s"`: 14.80 / 6.40 MB (−17%), 94 s. Both: 12.85 / 5.65 MB (−28%), 155 s. `opt-level = 2`: 17.15 MB (−4%). Sixteen codegen units: 19.42 MB (+8.5%), 97 s. Fat LTO costs only build time (73 s more there; run again later that day 143 → 210 s and 18.04 → 16.57 MB, boot 50.7 and 50.9 ms and layout 21.9 ms both ways over twelve alternating launches), and a Linux bake could say it as the Apple one now does, on Cargo's command line: a bake builds with `--target`, so it shares no artifact with the tools above. Sixteen codegen units under fat LTO, which on Apple build faster than today's profile, do not here: 193 s for 17.36 MB (−3.8%). `"s"` trades the hosts' speed, unmeasured there. Both are Charlie's to rule with the other platforms. An Apple production bake does not move the same way (M4, Caltrain's `ExactMac`, as built / with `strip -x`): thin LTO 11.98 / 9.06 MB; fat LTO 11.82 / 8.62 MB (−1% / −5%); `opt-level = "s"` 13.60 / 10.78 MB, larger by 14%, and its runner and layout at boot 7% slower (16.1 ms against 15.1). Fat LTO is built there (the bake says it for an Apple target, which no tool builds for; LLP 1036.000 §8); `"s"` is a loss. The Swift side has the same trade and it is also left: `-Osize` makes Sparkline's stripped binary 7.8% smaller (8.73 → 8.04 MB, 12.6% with fat LTO) and its first apply of 295 views 7% slower (17.9 ms against 16.7, sixteen alternating launches).
- **A cold bake of an app with a GPU crate is two Cargo builds end to end: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Cargo's `--timings`, Caltrain on the ten-core M4, before the registry crates were kept; LLP 1036.000 §7). The GPU crate first, 39 s for 146 unit-seconds, because it is one chain (naga 14 s → wgpu-core 13 s → wgpu 7 s); then the app's archive, 49 s for 366, whose chain is the kernel (33 s) and the app (3.5 s). They are in sequence because the app's build script binds the GPU product (LLP 1009 D6), and nothing else can warm the app's dependencies meanwhile: asked for by another selection they are other units (`-p exact-apple` recompiles 25 crates in a warm target, the kernel among them; with the GPU crate, 32). Overlapping them needs the GPU crate in its own target directory and the app's dependencies compiled before its build script may run, which is a change to what `buildBake` promises on every platform. It is worth less than the chains suggest on ten cores, where ExactKit's compile already fills the GPU phase's idle ones (the three together are 660 CPU-seconds, 66 s at best), and more on eighteen. In a checkout that starts from the kept registry crates (§11) the two builds are 12.9 s (wgpu-hal 3 s → wgpu-core 9 s → wgpu 5 s, then ours) and 37 s (the kernel, 30 s), so overlapping them is worth at most those 13 s of a 58 s first build.
- **What a second checkout still compiles** (2026-10-04; LLP 1036.000 §7, §10, §11). The host's two Rust modules and the registry crates under an app are now kept for the machine (`~/.cache/exact/apple-modules`, `~/.cache/exact/apple-crates`): a second checkout's first build is 78 → 58 s on an M4 and 38 crates, all of them path crates or standing on one. What is left, in the order it costs: the kernel (33 s in one rustc front end, the chain every build waits on); `wgpu-core` and `wgpu`, registry crates rebuilt in every checkout because the vendored `wgpu-hal` under them is a path crate (20 s of the GPU crate's build; a patched crate consumed as a registry or git source would be kept like the rest); ExactKit (150 CPU-seconds, beside the bake, and not what the Rust waits for: at a lower priority the build is the same, 57.7–58.9 s against 57.3–58.6). The kernel alone is 12.6 s on an idle eighteen-core M5, 10 s of it rustc and 7 of those LLVM over its sixteen codegen units, so it is the cores and not the front end. sccache was tried on the Linux host and is not it (95 → 79 s): what it cannot share is the same path crates.
- **A binary that takes updates ships every loaded module** (2026-10-04; LLP 1047 D8, "after the web target"; LLP 1036.000 §8). A production Apple build at store level 0 now leaves out each of the four its plan cannot reach (the bake's `loads`: the Canvas 2D GPU module, the SVG island module, the video and web arms; Sparkline's Mac bundle 16.3 → 11.6 MB), because that plan is the only one the binary will ever run. A binary at any other level carries all four (4.7 MB on a Mac) for a later bundle that might reach one, and dropping them there needs the linked set as a compatibility input, so a bundle that reaches a module its binary lacks is refused before it is applied (D6, D8).
- **An abort a listener suppresses leaves a browser-realm fetch pending until its reply** (2026-10-04, both families' round-5 reviews of the app-diary fixes, 4a8). Hermes watches a fetch's signal through Ibex's abort hooks; the wasm target's browser module realm loads prelude.js alone, so `watchAbort` is an `addEventListener` an earlier `stopImmediatePropagation` skips, and the fetch rejects only when its reply lands (fulfill rechecks `signal.aborted`). The default web build (the JS target) uses the browser's own fetch and is unaffected.
- **A translucent raster is darker on macOS than in Chrome** (2026-10-04, fix/images, seen in screenshots only). A 1×1 PNG of `rgba(255,0,0,0.5)` scaled to 40 pt paints pink over white in Chrome and a near-solid red in the macOS host (shown from a `data:` URL; a file asset with alpha not yet compared). Measure the pixels on macOS, iOS and Linux against Chrome's, then look at `RasterImage.decode`'s alpha handling (premultiplied or not) and the draw's blend.
- **A Rust source in a logic module is asked without its `Target`** (files F22, 2026-10-04, read in `logic/abi`, not reproduced). The ABI's ops 3/4 call `answer`/`parse`, whose defaults drop to `query` (the bake placeholder); in process (`Mixed`, `Storage`) the runner calls `answer_for`/`parse_for`. A source that parks a call by `Target` therefore answers its placeholder, silently, on the JS web target only. Carry the target across the ABI (both hosts' encoders, an ABI version), or refuse at load a module whose source overrides `answer_for` and not `answer`. No longer needed for `doc:` paths, which TypeScript reaches now (F2).
- **`contract types`' `Sources` demands the Rust half's sources from `app.ts`** (files F21, 2026-10-04). In a mixed app the generated `Sources` names every source, so the TypeScript half writes `Omit<Sources, …>` by hand. The composition that knows which sources are Rust's (`RUST_SOURCES`, `build_mixed_with`) is Rust code; `app.json` could name them, or the bake could emit a `TypeScriptSources` type from the composer it already runs.
- **Pointer events, what is left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1056](llp/1056-canvas-2d.rfc.md) §8.6; the paint diary's F1). No `coalesced` list: a stroke is a polyline at the display's rate, and DOM's `getCoalescedEvents()` as a field needs a second record type (a record cannot list itself in the plan's types). An iPad's hovering pointer and pencil send no free `pointermove` (UIKit's hover recognizer). Linux delivers every move, as its `pan` does, not one a frame. The Mac agent's held drags read `pressedMouseButtons` beside the event's own button; a synthesized pen is never a tablet event, so pen pressure on macOS is untested by script. `exact new`'s template now passes the module's canvas roster; apps made before it (x2apps) have to add `.with_canvas_surfaces(CANVAS_SURFACES)` to their `apple/` and `web/` crates by hand.
- **An empty date on iOS shows now** (2026-10-04, after kanban F23's macOS fix, `DateField` in `DatesMac.swift`): a compact `UIDatePicker` always holds a date, so `input type="date"` with no value shows today's (once cleared, the date it last held), where the web shows `mm/dd/yyyy` and macOS now its format as a placeholder. The value is `""` and `type "id" ""` clears it; only the picture is wrong.
- **CSS `outline`** (2026-10-04, x2apps spreadsheet F3): `outline`, `outline-width/-style/-color/-offset` are refused. On the web they would be CSS (and `outline: none` takes the browser's focus ring off a focusable grid); natively nothing draws a focus ring on a box, so they need paint rows outside the border box, following `border-radius`, above the content, on Apple and Linux. Admit with the four rows and their shorthand, painted on every host, and a `:focus-visible` answer (none exists for any row yet).
- **Clipboard events in native text fields** (2026-10-04, fix/a11y): `copy`, `cut` and `paste` reach a focused box on every host and a field on the web, but a macOS or iOS text field's or textarea's own editing fires none of them (the field editor and UITextField take the Edit actions). A field editor subclass (AppKit) and `UITextField`/`UITextView` overrides would deliver them before the platform's own paste.
- **Keys, what native hosts still lack** (2026-10-04, x2apps calc F3 / minesweeper F7; docs/contract-grammar.md#keys). `key` bubbles and `preventDefault()` claims a key on every host now, but on macOS and iOS a scroller does not scroll by arrow, Space or page keys (the web's default), and iOS carries no `aria-keyshortcuts` (`UIKeyCommand`s from the focused session's buttons, as `ShortcutsMac.swift` routes them). The iOS hardware-keyboard path (`TextField.pressesBegan`) is driven only through the agent's synthesized keys; no real keyboard has pressed it.
- **The JS target's data seam caps an answer at 16 MiB** (2026-10-04, LLP 1090 conformance). `rust-data.js` goes through the logic ABI, whose `MAX_MESSAGE` is `MAX_HOST_WORK_BYTES`; the runner's in-process data source has no cap, so an answer between 16 MiB and the runner's value bounds loads on wasm, native and Linux and fails on the JS target (docs/agent-pitfalls.md). Either bound answers alike on every host or let the JS seam carry what the runner does.
- **`host/web-js/render.mjs` and Rust data** (2026-10-04): it cannot load the server bundle of an app with Rust data (`admission.js`'s `import.meta.url` in an IIFE).
- **The JS jump lands what a timer sent at its last instant** (2026-10-04, LLP 1092 "As built"). `host/web-js/agent.js`'s `clock +N` waits for every reply after a stop and runs its `then`; the wasm glue, the Linux agent and Apple stop waiting once no timer is due by the jump's end, so a request a timer sends at exactly `to` lands there on real time and its `then` waits for the next operation. Conformance avoids it (`queue.contract`'s timer sends nothing); pick one behaviour and make the other hosts follow.
- **One-slot invariants of a queue mutation's slot** (2026-10-05, LLP 1092 D12, semantics/README.md "Queued sends and gated tasks"). `Reachable.slotIn` and its siblings are proved of slots no drain writes (`isQueue p x = false`). For a queue mutation's slot, carry the property of the answers its waiting sends will land (`∀ q ∈ c.queued, q.1 = x → ∀ v, V (.some v)`) through `runAction` and `nextCommit`. Lean's `pending(m)` of a waiting queue is false (the runner's is true): model it if a program needs it.
- **What the Lean semantics still leaves out** (2026-10-04, semantics/README.md). Routes landed; `t(...)`, the `format*` entries, geometry reads, frame tasks, a mutation's `then`, form-control payloads and the `navigate` event remain. `difftest explore apps/*/app.contract` reports Caltrain (`formatTime`), Messages (frame tasks) and RealWorld (`then`) as outside it. A virtualized list's rows are left out of the observation (the runner windows them by layout), so a scripted tap on one is not compared meaningfully.
- **tvOS, what is owed** (2026-10-03, landed from Doug Lowder's `doug/tvos`; LLP 1008 §9 "tvOS"). iOS-only files that shared code names break it (twice in one landing: `PointerIOS.swift`, then `NavigationTitleIOS.swift` and `GroupedListIOS.swift`); the tier 2 async lane (`scripts/async.mjs --tier 2`, hourly) now builds tvOS and files what breaks, but nothing starts that lane on fleet hardware yet. `scripts/agent.mjs` has no tvOS target (it installs the iOS build), so `smoke.mjs` cannot drive a TV, and nothing here presses the Siri Remote by script (`simctl`/`devicectl` have no route; Doug drove it with Argent). No device builds, icons (layered brand assets) or Top Shelf. A grouped list's toggle row shows a checkmark but Select does not flip it. Color Gallery's `apps/color-gallery/apple/build.rs` still maps tvOS to macOS: `bun host/apple/build.mjs --tvos color-gallery-apple --run` compiles, then fails with "actual bake receipt names another target, platform or app" (2026-10-06, while verifying Doug's Hermes shim fix); Weatherlight builds and runs on the same simulator.
- **A plan decoded inside wasm at activation** (2026-10-03, LLP 1029.000's per-byte fuel). Activation hands the data module the whole encoded plan, which it decodes at about 430 wasmi instructions a byte: Caltrain's 46 KB plan is about 20 million. Unmeasured: what that costs in wall time on the native interpreted path against the 100 ms cold-start budget. If it matters, activation gives the module only the tables it reads.
- **Hosts outside the repository** (2026-10-03, Charlie: "out-of-tree is for like Amazon's new TV operating system thing"; `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Surfaces). An RFC beside LLP 1036.001 (apps outside the repository): what a host with its own UI stack consumes from exact2 (the plan and kernel wire, the runner, the agent protocol), and what exact2 would have to publish for it (a stable host ABI, `host/web-js/conform.mjs` runnable outside this repository, drivers that do not assume `host/*`). Amazon's Vega OS is the motivating case; first find out what native surface its SDK gives an app.
- **A standing web competitor bench** (2026-10-04, `~/bench/dioxus`). The perf scoreboard measures iOS against SwiftUI and UIKit and never the web or a bounded window's shift.
