One afternoon against Dioxus Web found the feed shipped three times (cf12cb9ae), the whole-list tap (now a development-build warning, `host/web-js/seam.js`), a lazy source's first-shift hitch, and the clamp bug that threw readers back 55–59 rows at every window shift on web, macOS and iOS (ca83e4eaa, 04c1822ec). The harness (`web/bench.mjs`: load, bytes, heap, scroll at four speeds, taps; `web/progress.mjs`: content progress by row) and the Dioxus port (`heavy-dx/`) are there: give them a run on each landing that touches the web host or collections, beside the iOS series.
- **The Vello canvas keeps every decoded image** (2026-10-04, Blitz's crash). Dioxus Native's default renderer (vello_hybrid 0.0.9) panics with `AtlasLimitReached` after about 9,000 pt of a feed of 104 distinct photos. `canvas/vello/src/lib.rs`'s `images()` map holds every decoded image by the host's handle and nothing evicts it except `ecg_trim` (a memory warning on Apple). Whether `exact-vello`'s atlas behind it is bounded is untested. Drive a Canvas 2D surface through a few hundred distinct images and watch memory and errors.
- **Sub-second Rust edits without hot-patching** (2026-10-05, [LLP 1029.000.001](llp/1029.000.001-sub-second-rust-edits.rfc.md), Draft, awaiting Charlie). A one-line data-crate edit costs 2.3–3.2 s on the JS target's dev loop and 1.6–2.0 s with live replacement on native; the crate compiles in 0.13–0.55 s ([LLP 1029.000.000](llp/1029.000.000-hot-patching-rust-measured.research.md)). The RFC builds the dev module in `logic-dev`, swaps the module in the page for a Rust-only edit, and builds only the variants connected clients run, in parallel; Subsecond-style function patching was weighed and declined.
- **Linux: Blink breaks in ordinary paragraphs** (LLP 1085 F3). cosmic-text breaks URLs after `/` where Chrome doesn't (3–5% of real rows); the textflow walker already carries Blink's table on the flow path.
- **A drawn switch on the web** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1084](llp/1084-native-grouped-lists.rfc.md) §4). Chrome has no `switch` attribute and draws a checkbox (LLP 1069.001 D1). A 51 × 31 track in `index.html` under `@supports not selector(::thumb)` is a few lines. But it moves the switch's web box, so it also needs the Chrome-oracle table in `kernel/tests/it/browser_controls.rs` re-recorded and `ControlKind::Switch`'s default size changed from 13 × 13.
- **Grouped lists, what is left** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1084](llp/1084-native-grouped-lists.rfc.md) §4, §7). The large-title collapse follows the route's authored scroll, which a grouped list hides: `setContentScrollView` should take the collection view. A grouped list's rows take no `swipeContent` (the list configuration's own swipe providers would). The Signal Clone's `Section`/`Row`/`ActionRow` components can become a grouped list (its repository). `sidebar` styles wait for an iPad or Mac consumer.
- **Styleable materials** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q3). A `style` class cannot carry `backgroundMaterial` or `glassGroup` because they are props; LLP 1069.011 D12 already makes `buttonStyle` a styleable prop, and these two can join it. WebKit's `-apple-visual-effect` is not a browser name under LLP 1081 D1 (it is behind `useSystemAppearance`), so there is nothing to adopt.
- **Style names and their kinds in `schema.json`** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q5). LLP 1081 puts the author style names, with provenance, in Contract's `style_names.rs`. Moving them into the schema is a mapping design, not two new fields: names and rows are many to many (`gap` sets two rows, `translate` sets `Translate` and `TranslateZ`, `flex` sets three different values).
- **CamelCase for Contract's kebab-case props** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q6). `scroll-start`, `scroll-restoration`, `initial-item-count` and `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height` look like CSS properties and are props; camelCase, Contract's spelling for its own props, would make a kebab-case name always mean CSS or an HTML attribute. Ask Charlie first about `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height`, his "for now" spelling.
- **What `tree --ax` found on macOS and iOS** (2026-10-03, LLP 1080.002 §7). AppKit exposes the accessibility fixture's `aria-hidden` decoration and the button inside its `inert` column (`exposed-hidden` ×2, LLP 1001's "AppKit/Linux subtree enforcement remains open"), and its checkbox and password field are exposed through cells that answer no name (the label is set on the control view; whether VoiceOver speaks it is unverified). On iOS a `role="img"` column exposes its inner button and no image (the web exposes `image "Chart"`). Not built: a projected segment's join to its tab, and the macOS window toolbar's walk (both reported as coverage limits). On iOS the read needs UIKit's accessibility runtime: `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.apple.Accessibility ApplicationAccessibilityEnabled -bool true` on a simulator, then relaunch; the driver could set it for an `ax` drive.
- **LLP 1079 on hardware, what is unrun** (2026-10-03, `perf`'s §As built). Linux's display-loop sampler and `SIGUSR1` trace were type-checked for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`, never run on a DRM display. iOS Save Trace was clicked on a simulator (2026-10-05: saved, Share… offers the file, `agent.mjs trace --phone <simulator>` copied it from the container and read it); on a phone neither the share sheet's AirDrop nor the driver's `devicectl` copy has been run, and macOS's ⌥⌘T was not clicked. The `--stress-url` p95 comparison, measuring on and off, was not run.
- **The JS target's development `perf` costs about 3.7 µs a commit** (2026-10-03, LLP 1079 §As built). 800 caltrain hover commits took 9.7 ms against 6.7 ms with `perf.js` stubbed out. Two-thirds is the mutation observer that gives `authored`/`created`/`retired`, the rest the `pf` wrappers; production carries neither. A cheaper count would need `rt.js` to stamp its own writes, which every production bundle would then carry.
- **Eleven `exact-linux` content-region tests fail at 612d8038** (2026-10-03, found checking LLP 1079). The `retained_actions_*` tests in `presenter::swipe_tests` and `presenter::content_region_tests` fail with `content region layout: Layout(ContentRegion("exact-offer/source budget exhausted"))` in `swipe_tests::ready`. `exact-linux` is not a default member, so the five checks do not run it. The same eleven fail with and without LLP 1079.
- **JS-target grant parity, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed from `web/grants` (c4e02b00, 207c2c04, dcd938ed) after three review rounds; both final reviews still mark these). Unicode spellings the Rust parser accepts and the web treats differently: U+0085 in a module's grant text is refused at `host/web/module-glue.js` prepare; with a U+FEFF line a parser-accepted `auth.session` never installs on the JS target, and the wasm page's surface check admits a U+FEFF line native refuses. A `Response` with an empty URL (an opaque, `no-cors` answer) skips the final-origin check, so an ungranted redirect can succeed there. A redirect whose hop the browser itself rejects reports `Network`, not native's `Refused` (a declared deviation, LLP 1071 §7: the page cannot see the destination). A Rust-only page exposes its file commands before its grant set is installed. Two `Store` facade differences from native in `host/web-js/ts-data.js`. `apps/fieldnotes/web/tests/worker.rs` is not migrated to grant-set arguments. Out of scope by Charlie's ruling (grants on the web are parity, not a sandbox: the page's own code can always reach the browser's fetch): forged, self-sealed or accessor-backed grant objects, and app code replacing the fetch late host modules use.
- **Native bars, what Signal Clone still needs a module for** (2026-10-02, LLP 1075.003 in an outside app; its DIARY.md "build 5"; title views, `hidesBottomBarWhenPushed`, the barless route and content under the bar landed as §9.10). The tab bar's tint from the selected tab's authored `-exact-tint-color`; a symbol beside the heading in a drawn title (Signal's verified seal).
- **Held contacts on iOS** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1080.000](llp/1080.000-real-touches-on-ios.rfc.md) P3, §11). No touch stays down across requests: XCTest's public calls press and lift in one call, and its private event records lift at their end. Whole gestures are drivable now (`tap … drag`, §11: drags, holds, the edge pop, swipe to reply, with reads `during` the hold), but a move after a read, a reversal and the glass press with a screenshot mid-swell are not. Needs a continuation API in a later Xcode, or Charlie's call (Q2). Owed by §11: the native swipe-action reveal driven on Messages, and the edge-swipe fix on a phone.
- **A collection list has no "start at the end" mode** (2026-10-02, the Signal Clone app's transcript; LLP 1010 §6 says end-follow "only applies when the user was already at the end", and nothing positions a new collection there). A chat transcript opens bottom-anchored (Signal's `ConversationViewLayout` with exact sizes). On `list virtualized=true scrollFollowEnd=true`, the app needs `scrollTop=(1000000 + n)` to reach the end, which lands short by the estimate error of the rows it has not built (84 pt with `estimated-item-height=52`); the app then calls `scrollIntoView("transcript", lastKey, block="end")` after its first command lands. A first-class initial position (`scroll-start: end`, or `scrollFollowEnd` meaning "start following") that builds the last rows first and anchors the end through measurement would remove both.
- **Keyboard as a content inset, not a relayout** (2026-10-02, the Signal Clone app, `~/.tuft/projects/signal-exact2/DIARY.md` "scrolling"). With `interactive-widget="resizes-content"` every keyboard frame, including each frame of an interactive dismissal, resizes the root and the kernel lays out the whole screen. Signal-iOS pins its toolbar to `keyboardLayoutGuide` and changes only the collection view's `contentInset.bottom` (skipped while dragging; at-bottom stays at bottom). A mode that overlays the keyboard and gives the app a `keyboard-inset` env() that the host applies as the scroller's `contentInset` would keep the transcript's layout untouched.
- **Animated scroll to the end on send, what is left** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone; iOS, macOS and the web host built 2026-10-03, LLP 1070.000 §11). Linux and the JS web target land a smooth correction at once. A plain `scroll` still jumps on a `scrollTop` write followed to the end.
- **Smooth collection jumps cross unbuilt rows** (2026-10-03, astra's review of the smooth-correction hosts, `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-03-signal-gaps.astra.md` #5). While a smooth correction animates, every host reports the destination: a mid-flight report told the runner that the reader had left the end, and that stopped the end-follow. So a smooth `scrollIntoView` farther than the mounted window crosses spacers no row fills. A fix needs the report to carry both the physical port, which is what to realize, and the destination, which is what to anchor. That is a wire change in `CollectionFacts`. A send's end-follow is within the window and never shows it.
- **A symbol image laid out at 0×0 after first layout, not reproduced** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone DIARY "build 4"; retried 2026-10-03). A composer camera or mic `symbol:` image that reappears after a send, with no explicit `width`/`height`, measured 0×0 while the app re-asked a resource every 250 ms. On current main it does not reproduce: four agent drives (`--timing platform`, send, layouts at 0/250/500/900 ms and after settle) give 26.33×20.33 every time. Real keyboard input is not drivable here (axe types into nothing without focus). The one path found by reading, never shown to be hit: `PresenterIOS.flushIntrinsicSizes` drops a queued symbol size whose `loadGeneration` moved on, and `loadImage` bumps the generation without re-queuing when `updateSymbol` finds the same `symbolKey`. The clone keeps explicit sizes. Next step: a real-touch drive (LLP 1080.000) of type, send and reappear, logging any dropped intrinsic.
- **Per-corner radii as a shape mask, not a CPU draw** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone). A box whose four corner radii differ (a chat bubble's 18/4 pt cluster corners) sets `boxDrawn` and paints its fill in `draw(_:)`, one CPU backing store per bubble, redrawn on every size change. Signal-iOS uses a solid colour or gradient view masked by a `CAShapeLayer` path (implicit animations off). `BoxLayerIOS` could do the same: layer background plus a shape-layer mask for non-uniform radii.
- **The web cores are over their ceilings since LLP 1077** (2026-10-02, measured as `metrics.mjs --long` does: `app.wasm` brotli-11, stages apart). At `220c2128e` Caltrain / RealWorld / video-player were 308.8 / 302.6 / 246.1 KiB; at `ef01dcadd` 329.3 / 324.0 / 266.6. Half of 1077's growth was std's float reader come back (`corner.rs`'s `superellipse()` read through `str::parse::<f32>()`, reached from every style write): read through `exact-num` since, 317.2 / 311.4 / 254.5, still 5.5–7.4 over 310 / 304 / 249. The rest is 1077's style codecs reaching every core through `StyleProps` (corner-shape, text and box shadows, rotate, stroke, the symbol palette: ~16 KB raw in a names build): link them by use as `gradients` and `effects` are, or raise the ceilings on purpose.
- **LLP 1075.003 (native platform control) owes** (2026-10-02, `lane/native-control`, §9.4). Stages 0–3 are built: hatches on an app's module (`navigation`, `route`, `routeEnded`, `tabs`, `tabContainer`) over Exact's own bar and retained tabs, declared `data-*`, host covers, write-on-change controls, multi-detent sheets, and a large title collapsing with its scroller. Owed: James's two cases at a pinned revision in his app; real touches (a finger on a bar item, a finger's collapse and pull to top, a held sheet drag, a minimizing tab bar); a cold launch filmed on a phone with and without hatches, and a memory warning; Stage 4 (TypeScript by experiment), which needs NativeScript's runtime, a dependency nobody approved; web and AppKit hatches; the `element` hatch and the named action (Stage 5, on demand); `back()` and `open(url)` on `ExactTabContents`; the development ownership check on an app's tab container. On the JS target `project` runs only from the router's effect, so a navigation root with no router slot is never projected there (its stacks, and now its panels, stay as authored); it predates the plan. LLP 1075.003.000 (hatches on every host, Stages 1–4 done; a hatched node in every row of the Extra Heavy feed costs a fling about 16% CPU and, on the iPhone, 5 fps, all of it the lost row reuse: the call and an unhatched build cost nothing measurable, §7.5) built the opt-in that takes that price back (`element.reusable`, §7.6); owes a development check on macOS and the web and for a base view's owned properties; hatches on the wasm web target, if a game or a fixture ever wants them; an LLP on which AppKit containers Exact projects.
- **A module view's input, twice** (2026-10-02, astra's delta review of the hatches' fixes). On both web targets a module view hears the page's own input as any element does (glue.js `attach`; rt.js `on` since e0ee5759f) and its module's reports (`event(code)`): a module whose own button reports `event(0)` for a click the page also sees delivers one press twice, and focus, blur and keys likewise. LLP 1024 should say which one a module view hears for each kind (the module's report only for what the page cannot see, or the module's only); Apple's hosts are the third to check.
- **Smoke checks that fail on main** (2026-10-02, seen at `220c2128e` and on the native-control landing alike). `smoke.mjs macos --app native-fixture`: "Tab reaches the module's editing descendant" (the responder stays `NodeView`, `editor` null). `smoke.mjs web --app recorder` (the JS target, seen at `ef01dcadd`): "the status is coarse, and answered by native.later" counts the wasm runner's `request N (status): POST exact-native:` journal lines, which the JS runtime does not write, so it counts 0; and `conform.mjs recorder` fails `tap toggle` and the next step because Chrome leaves the toggle focused on the JS page and not on the wasm page. Under load (the five checks beside a simulator or another build), `exact-update`'s store tests fail now and then and pass alone: `store::a_lower_seq_is_refused` (`update/tests/it/support.rs:230`), `store::a_head_naming_the_embedded_plan_and_assets_is_current_and_downloads_nothing` (`update/tests/it/store.rs:897`), `durability::an_unchanged_asset_is_stored_once_and_old_entries_are_collected`.
- **Kept answers: repeated activation and slow first answers** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1027.005](llp/1027.005-resource-identity-and-request-context.rfc.md) D4/D5). Two issues reproduced during review, neither part of the `with` landing. (1) Consecutive `data_ready()` calls before an asynchronous reply issued tickets 1 and 2: a forced ask bypasses `enqueue`'s identical-request rule. No host calls it twice today (Apple and Linux guard with `data_activated`, wasm activates once per boot), so the fix is small and only for a future host. (2) Kept answers are admitted only for a source unready at boot and then remain visible while its asynchronous reply is pending: on the JS runtime, Fieldnotes's real TypeScript source showed the bake's "Opening your notebook…" until a controlled SQLite reply, and the runner does the same for any source ready at boot. The JS target also keeps nothing for a Rust module loaded after first paint. Whether a consumer needs either closed is follow-up 3 of the RFC.

- **Web motion followers** (2026-10-03). The release-follower lifecycle bug is fixed by a targeted change: post-commit reconciliation cancels a follower as soon as its consumer no longer resolves to that source. `web/motion2` (shelved on expo-build-1000) tried and abandoned compositor followers for CSS-eased sources: reproducing CSS animation semantics in retimed followers kept diverging, so main's per-frame seeking stays for that case. Still owed (the final review, codex sol): the real-plan test's later samples are corrected by the agent clock itself (each `clock` op reconciles timelines on both web targets), so it would stay green if the handed-off per-frame callback stopped running; add a check that lets the page's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop run without agent clock operations between samples.
- **Agent web drives, what the no-GPU fix left** (2026-10-02): `screenshot` settles the GPU before its two frame waits and image wait, not after; the JS target's agent settle does not await the GPU module's first import; `surfaceWork` ignores a pending `settled()`; `fallbackApplied` survives a successful module swap. None is the user-facing hang (fixed); each is a narrower race in the agent's drive.
- **JS dev reload carry, what is left** (2026-10-02): the logic revision hashes the app chunk and `app.module.wasm` only, so a native page module served at a stable URL (`./modules/index.js`) can change without dropping settled answers; a checkpoint is deleted before its page is written, so a retried GET boots fresh silently; the route-stack assertion would pass with router carry removed; a blurred autofocus field can take focus again after a carried reload (plausible); Caltrain's edit-to-frame is 273 ms p50 against the 100 ms budget (it was over before the carry).
- **Web test freshness and prerequisites from the build** (2026-10-02, `web/webtests` b8b93ed5, reverted before landing). One source of truth (`build.mjs --check` running the build's own checks; freshness roots from Cargo's dependency closure) is the right design, but that commit made the check a mandatory preflight that runs Cargo before a game's `.shells` workspace exists, breaking every clean game web build, and it still missed the render binary's and GPU crates' inputs and deleted inputs. `CHROME=''` is still taken as a browser path.
- **Firefox/WebKit carrier fidelity** (2026-10-02, the async `conform-firefox`/`conform-webkit` steps): keys go to an iframe guest before Chrome's browser-owned path and non-button keys take a different route than Chrome's; a wheel moves the pointer first (hover becomes part of the input, so the baselined RealWorld scroll gap is not a pure wheel-delta difference); a root with a `testId` double-counts document scroll; the public session advertises held contacts the carrier refuses; synthetic plans still compile after a launch failure. WebKit runs on a Mac (Linux lacks 36 system libraries and the builders have no sudo): first run 2026-10-02 on the M5 mini (WebKit 26.6, `bunx playwright@1.63.0 install webkit`, Chrome for Testing 153 as the oracle) matched Chrome's state on all six apps, 0 failures against `known-webkit.json` (a button is not focused by a click, native input widths). Real Safari through `safaridriver` stays optional: it needs `safaridriver --enable` once, by an admin.
- **CSS grid on the web, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed with the web program: every grid row lowers on both web targets, the kernel's grid grammar parses through cssparser, and `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` holds browser boxes to the kernel's with `--linux`). The final review's leftovers: a slot-bound grid value containing `var()`/`calc()` for placement, `grid-auto-flow` or `justify-items` is applied by the JS page but cleared by the kernel (track lists are checked); an auto-placed named span (`auto / span 2 foo`) lays out as `span 1` natively (Taffy resolves named spans only against an explicit line — declare it in LLP 1001 or patch Taffy); grids that resolve past Taffy's 10,000 tracks (`repeat(auto-fill, 1px)` in a wide box, `10000 / span 2`) are not refused; the JS range check splits serialized CSS on spaces, so an escaped numeric line name (`\31 99999`) is refused there.
- **Form controls, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed: a control in block flow is shrink-to-fit as in Chrome, 960 recorded Chrome cases; Caltrain's station Back button's tap area is its label now, as on the web). Open: a Pressable whose `href` is set or cleared after creation is restyled by the kernel as the other element while the page keeps its first tag (`<button>`/`<a>` is chosen once); an authored `box-sizing` on a checkbox or switch is ignored (forced border-box); `appearance: none` is still measured as the native widget; input types the kernel has no control for (`color`, `number`, `month`, `week`, `hidden`) still lower as text fields while the page makes the real widget; on the JS target the reset's `input[type=…] { appearance: auto }` outranks an authored `.cN { appearance: none }` (specificity), so the author's `none` loses (this predates the change). Caltrain's station search field is 20 characters wide in its card on every host (the text-field rule of 2026-09-30); if it should fill the card, its plan says `width="100%"`.
- **`smoke.mjs web` does not pass on Linux** (2026-10-02, expo-build-1000, Chrome 154 for Testing): scroll limits come from Mac fonts (expected 652, Linux 612), the canvas reference is Mac's, and Chrome there has no working WebGPU adapter even on SwiftShader. Judge the web smoke on a Mac; a Linux variant would need its own references.
- **A fold while editing can end the editing** (2026-10-02, lane/duo-kinks2; `bun scripts/smoke.mjs duo` on a fresh iPhone Duo, iOS 27.1): once in two full runs, `combos-half→0°: the title, the draft and the focus survived` failed with `noteFocused false` — the title input in the pushed route's sheet lost its focus when the half pose folded to the closed panel with the keyboard up; the text survived, and the same fold kept the focus at every other pose; on a second fresh Duo (lane/duo-kinks-land, 2026-10-02 23:27) it was the `insets` fixture's input instead, `insets-closed: focus and text survive a fold to 180°` with `focused false`, once in the run. The panel switch moves the window scene; look at what resigns the first responder there (`Presenter.editing`, the route controller's view leaving the old panel's window) before the next hinge fix.
- **Android memory after a crypto fling, where it goes** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10 Pro XL, exact2 320 vs Views 196 MB PSS: graphics 200 vs 153, native heap 76 vs 31, Java 19–21 vs 6). `dumpsys gfxinfo`: Skia's GPU cache holds 88 MB of scratch surfaces in ~12,600 entries for exact2 against 62 MB in ~5,900 for Views (37 vs 12 MB purgeable); marking single-draw animated layers non-overlapping (`setHasOverlappingRendering(false)`) did not change it, so it is not the reader's opacity layers; candidates are rounded-rect/path clips (row clips, avatars) needing mask surfaces, the dashed sparkline re-recorded each draw-in frame, and even-odd `RING` paths. Native heap (heapprofd): HWUI's Vulkan pipelines 12.6 MB and textures ~9 MB (more distinct draw kinds than Views'), exact2's own ~16 MB (settled data values 5, plan 2.75, layout leaves 1.7, rows 1.2, shaping ~1.5). The Java heap is not the stream copies (recycling them left it unchanged).
- **StyleProps copies: box its rarely used row groups** (2026-10-05, from `android/cpu2`; not done: it changes the shared generated schema while other lanes edit it). Main grew StyleProps from 1,592 to 1,816 bytes (grid tracks and placement, `env_resolved`, gradients, filters), and every whole-style copy pays for it: apply's `SetStyle` (clone, patch, intern), `svg::scene`'s `cascade` (one copy an SVG item a paint), `computed_style` callers that need many rows. Boxing the groups few nodes set (`Option<Box<GridRows>>`, the same for filters, gradients, env) in `kernel/build.rs`'s StyleProps generation would make the common copy ~1 KB smaller and its clone allocation-free for most nodes. The single-row reads already moved to `computed_source`/`computed_row`/`NodeArena::text_style` on `android/cpu2`.
- **Android CPU after `android/cpu2`** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10a, 6 runs a variant against Views in one session; cells are process CPU ms/s, exact / Views). Landed: the text cache's per-paint trim walks only when a budget could be over; a list row holding a scroller (easy's swipe-to-delete) is a kept row while the scroller stays put (every easy paint used to record every row that showed again); symbol pictures take their em square before their commit's layout (no second layout per row built); symbol paths parsed once; a symbol's font size read as one row, not a copied style; image visibility indexes the painted boxes once a paint; the reader (bench patch `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-reader.patch`) draws a dashed stroke as its path's segments, and a layer's draw-in dash reads and measures its path once, not every frame (crypto's main thread −25% at 12k). easy fling 1k 499/643, 3k 783/605, 6k 636/560, 12k 683/684, 24k 754/913 (before: 562, 683, 975, 776, 986; the `exact` thread 63–274 from 91–334 ms/s; Mcycles/s now below Views at every speed but 3k). crypto fling 760/714, 836/734, 985/717, 1064/773, 1285/947; crypto rest 713/655. Left: (1) a row costs ~2.8 M instructions on crypto (a big-core probe, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/crypto/probe`): binding evaluation in the VM (the chart's 48-point `points`, ~8%; decode ~20% of that), Taffy flexbox over five flex containers a row and text measured twice (max-content, then the definite width) after shaping (~20%), apply and its style cascade (~9%), motion's restatement of renewed nodes (~5%), the row's recording (~15%); Views' crypto row is a hand laid-out ViewGroup. (2) RenderThread runs on the little cores for whole segments (200–390 ms/s there against ~240 on the mid cores; Views' rarely does): the main thread's frames are short, so HWUI's hints ask for less; pinning it to the mid and big cores (tried: `fast_cores` by tid) kept it there but at lower clocks, mixed in ms/s, not kept. (3) crypto rest: the tick path is ~45% of the `exact` thread (the source's list copy, the old list's drop, `update_data`'s two passes over 5,000 items, conformance); a source that names the rows it changed would remove most of it (the runner takes `Changed { list, positions }` beside a value and skips the compare and rekey for the rest).
- **Android Canvas host against Views, by thread, on Parley** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL, ms/s exact - Views). crypto 6k: exact thread +255, RenderThread +27..40, hwuiTask +46..80, main -27; 12k: exact +350, RenderThread +29..43, hwuiTask +27, main -74. heavy 6k: exact +174, RenderThread +25, decode level (63 vs ~70 of the Views app's dispatcher and upload threads), main -59; 12k: exact +259, RenderThread +38, main -84. The gap is the exact thread less what the main thread saves; nothing else is worth a tenth of it. Probe shares of that thread at 6k-12k dp/s on Parley: layout 18.6% (48% of it text measuring, 22% shaping new strings, which paint needs anyway), the rebind's bindings 17% (the chart's `points` in the VM 6.3%; its parse at paint is 2.3%, so a parse cache buys little), recording rows 14% (the `svg` 6.4%), apply 7%, other commit effects 11%. RenderThread's excess on crypto: text placed again each frame (drawTextBlob 2.4 times the Views app's: Skia reuses a run's glyph placement only across whole-pixel moves, and the feed moves by fractions) and more path masks made (the software path renderer strokes 1.5 times as many contours; why is not found: the pulse ring and dot as rounded rects instead of four-cubic paths changed nothing). Rows and moved groups on whole pixels in the reader (`~/bench/lists/exact-c2v11/cpu2-snap.patch`) took 9-19 ms/s off RenderThread on both lists but added ~35 ms/s to heavy's main thread at 1k-6k, unexplained, so it is not merged.
- **Android Canvas host, a list row's cost while it scrolls: what the crypto probe shows** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/probe.sh`, `DP=1` for the bench driver's dp/s). After held spares and tracks with moves, at 6k-24k dp/s the exact thread is per-row work: layout 21% (the list is already the layout root and clean rows are cache hits; a rebound row is laid out whole because its five texts changed, ~7 measure callbacks a text of which ~3 reach the text engine, 45% of layout in measuring; a text whose box cannot change, nowrap in a stretched column, still dirties its ancestors), the rebind's bindings 18% (a third of it the VM building the chart's 48-point `points` string, which the kernel then parses again at paint), recording the row 14% (mostly the `svg`), apply 6.5%. Not tried: a row-local layout when a rebind changes no child order (the list's children change in the same commit, so the root is dirty); `points` as numbers end to end; recording only a rebound row's changed nodes (the recorder keeps a row whole, compared after it is walked). A report inside the window is still O(mounted rows) on both sides (the host sends every row's height each report, ~0.25 ms on a mid core, 80 a second).
- **`lead=1` on the Android reader (a window leading toward the travel) builds and destroys the lead every pass**: `refine_slice` resets the velocity to 0 when it returns, and the next unheld frame's pass (velocity 0) retires the rows the lead built (probe `VEL=1`: 2,093 rows built in 2 s at 24k px/s for 286 entering). Off by default, so no run measured it.
- **Android Canvas host, cold start: the first frame with content is three or four Vulkan pipelines** (easy on a Pixel 10 Pro XL: RenderThread 31-46 ms in `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, PowerVR compiling shaders, for FillRectOp, FillRRectOp, CircularRRectOp and text; the Views app's first frame is 11 ms). The reader draws a uniform rounded rect (Skia's CircularRRectOp) and one with unequal corners (FillRRectOp) differently: one op for both would drop a pipeline. The benchmark force-stops within 3 s, before HWUI saves its pipeline cache. The engine's boot is 18 ms of which half is the list's 10,000 items (decode 21%, `rekey_full` 27%, shape check 4%), but first content waits for the frame after the boot ends, so a shorter boot moves it only by whole frames.
- **Android GPU canvas in hardware buffers: what is owed** (2026-10-07; the reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-gpubuffers.patch`, against bench e6e9118). Run on PowerVR (Pixel 10 Pro XL) only. Owed: (1) a run on Mali and Adreno with a screenshot of a shader row: the buffer is rendered by wgpu's Vulkan device and sampled by HWUI's with no queue-family transfer to `VK_QUEUE_FAMILY_FOREIGN_EXT` and no fence, only wgpu's word that the submission completed, which PowerVR accepts; `gpubuf=0` is the window path. (2) The reader's fallback when buffers are refused (`canvasFrame` -2: it swaps the element's view for a TextureView) has never run. (3) A frame is published at the exact thread's next frame after its GPU work completes and drawn at the reader's next: up to a frame later than the window path showed it. (4) What it bought is small (Extra Heavy 1k 386 -> 362 mW, 3k 393 -> 379): with canvases off entirely Extra Heavy is ~340 mW at 1k, so most of a canvas's cost is its own render and `vkQueueSubmit` (a third of the exact thread while one shows; PowerVR's `RGXKickTA` and fence merges in the call). The lever left is not submitting from the exact thread at all: the shader in the reader's pass (AGSL from the same source), or a submit thread.
- **`-exact-animation-trigger` on the hosts that do not have it** (2026-10-06, [LLP 1055](llp/1055-svg-shapes-and-css-animations.rfc.md) D13). `view` is the default on Linux and Android. Owed: run it on Apple (the held play reaches its engine through the same `motion_sync`; nobody has looked at a device); build it on the web's JS target (pause a row mounted out of the port, resume on intersection) and the wasm glue (`Kernel::animation_row`, `revealed`); a reveal with nothing else to say is a commit of its own, which crypto's probe shows (+8% of the exact thread's instructions with `view`, most of it the animations now drawn on screen): carry it on the next commit instead if a profile says the commit is the cost.
- **crypto and xheavy energy on the Pixel 10 Pro XL, what was tried** (2026-10-06, `bin/energy.py`, CPU+GPU rails). Fixed: a collection pass took the feed's whole snapshot twice to count the rows it built (+2% of crypto's probe since `android/xblank`). Not the cause or not worth it: (1) crypto's `hwuiTask` threads at 3k-24k (Skia's software path masks, ~250-340 ms/s against Views' ~150-200; ~10 at 1k) are the charts' draw-in: a new path a frame for every visible row. The animation starts at mount, a viewport ahead, so rows show the last half of it (long paths, ~625 us of masks a frame against Views' ~345, which shows the first half) and none of it at 1k. Drawing the drawn part as round-capped `drawLines` removes the masks but puts +180 ms/s on RenderThread and triples CPU power at 3k-6k (315 -> 960 mW): RenderThread time is the expensive kind. Skipping recordings under half a pixel of dash travel cut the masks 14% at 3k with nothing measurable in mW; not kept. (2) `fast=0` (the exact thread on any core) and `boost=0` (no ADPF hint) change neither mW nor frames on crypto. (3) xheavy at 1k: the shader canvas's wgpu submit and present are a third of the exact thread (~80 ms/s) and the GPU rail is ~90 mW against Views' ~58; a canvas out of view already draws nothing (skipping by viewport changed nothing). Drawing a GPU module's canvas in the reader's own pass is still the lever.
- **Android list CPU after `android/cpu2`'s structural pass** (2026-10-06, Pixel 10a; the bench reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-hwpictures.patch`). Done: StyleProps' 20 rare rows behind one shared pointer (`"rare": true` in schema.json), a decode under way finishing into the cache, JPEG decoded at its shown size by the platform, and platform-decoded pictures decoded straight into the `AHardwareBuffer` the reader wraps (`hardwarePictures`/`imageBuffer`; a heavy fling run made and filled ~1,900 buffers on the exact thread for 106 decodes, because the reader's copy went ten paints after a picture left). Tried and not worth it: a memo of apply's set-style patches (−0.1% of crypto's probe), the SVG cascade's clone (~0.3%), cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache` (−0.6% of heavy's probe: its words rarely repeat within the cache's life). Left, heavy at 24k against Views (~1,150 vs ~810 ms/s): the exact thread and main together (~530 vs Views' main ~290: layout 20% of the exact thread, text measure 12% of it; paint 16%; `sync_images_moved` 11%, of which a third is its own index rebuild at every commit and a third `Images::poll` walking every view), RenderThread (~230–330 vs ~200), and the decode threads (~150–350 vs ~70: the same libjpeg work a picture, plus the bilinear scale to the shown size, 17% of them; Views' loader lets the GPU scale). PNG pictures and canvases still take the copy route. `android/xblank`'s time-budgeted refine costs crypto's and easy's probes +2% (3.462 → 3.536 G instructions on crypto); not looked into.
- **Android CPU after `android/cpu3`: what is left on heavy and xheavy** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10 Pro XL, same-session runs against Views; the bench reader's changes are `~/bench/logs-cpu3/bench-cpu3.patch`). Found and fixed: MapLibre's "Worker" threads (~1,900 ms/s at 24k against Views' ~430) were the map module's `MapLibreMap.clear()` on every rebind, which makes MapLibre lay out every loaded tile again; removing its own markers instead brings them to Views' level (bench `NativeMap.kt`). An animated GPU canvas made a `VkImageView` and `VkFramebuffer` every frame (PowerVR sets up and tears down a render target for each: `vkmem_free` ~90 ms/s), staged its uniform through a transfer job, and present submitted a PRESENT→PRESENT barrier alone (wgpu-hal EXACT-PATCHES 6–7, `FrameUniform` on Vulkan). Results: xheavy fling CPU 1k/12k/24k 1526/2664/4134 → 1317/2145/2740 ms/s (Views 797/1524/2168), rest 1278 → 984 (Views 634). Left: (1) xheavy fps at 24k is ~2 lower (110 → 108; Views 78) and 12k ~1 lower: the main thread does the same Mcycles at ~2.4 GHz instead of ~2.7 now that MapLibre's waste no longer holds the cluster's clock up (DVFS); a per-frame cut on main/RenderThread (WebView construction, native-view recording) would buy it back. (2) The shader canvas still costs the exact thread ~130–170 ms/s at 120 Hz (one wgpu submit + present a frame through PowerVR's kernel driver, the acquire thread ~30, `vk_tlsem4_bg` ~18) and RenderThread the TextureView's layer update; Views draws it as AGSL inside HWUI's pass — drawing a GPU module's canvas in the reader's RenderThread pass is the remaining lever. (3) Dashed circles (the live row's rings) are drawn as arcs by the reader now (HWUI had stroked them in software every frame, hwuiTask ~70 ms/s); other dashed or concave paths still go through Skia's software path renderer. (4) heavy: pictures are resampled to the size they show at and shared across sizes, 12 viewports kept (decodes 330 → 184 a fling; CPU at 12k/24k 1217/1576 → 1103/1344 ms/s, Views 740/900; slow speeds unchanged, 1k 668 vs Views 570; end PSS by `Debug.MemoryInfo` ~715 → ~525 MB, Views ~485); what remains is per-row construction on the exact thread (taffy layout, paint, collection feedback, `sync_images_moved` ~12% at 24k), the picture hand-off (a `HardwareBuffer` made and copied into on the exact thread, ~12% of main+exact at 24k; decoding straight into one would drop the copy and the Rust-side duplicate), and Views' main thread doing its binding alone. (5) The bench's `pss_all_mb` (compact `dumpsys meminfo` after the run) is bimodal for heavy (~270 or ~600 MB on the same build, by whether the dump lands before HWUI's graphics release); compare `driver.json`'s `pssEndMB`.
- **Android memory on the Nothing (4a) Pro after `android/mem`** (2026-10-05, `dumpsys meminfo` App Summary at the end of a fling; tools and the bench-copy patch in `~/bench/android/results/mem-patch`). Crypto 97 vs Views 55 MB before: the library's resident pages ~17 MB (Views' code is shared and counts ~1 MB), native heap + mimalloc 36 vs 15, Java 8 vs 9–17, graphics equal. Landed: the Canvas host lets go of its library's read-only pages 2 s after boot (`android::release_library_pages`): .rodata 6.6 -> 0.7 MB resident (the baked market and coins.json are read once), CPU, fps and cold start unchanged. With the bench build's order below, medians of three on 268878a98: crypto fling 96.9 -> 90.1 MB (Views 55.6), daily 0.1–8.7 MB lower per scenario but still 2–13 over Views, xheavy within its ±20 MB noise and under Views (418–428 vs 456). In the bench build only (`order-clang`, `order.txt`): lld's `--symbol-ordering-file` puts the functions a run executes first, then boot-only ones, from an instrumented (`-Cprofile-generate`) build's counters (boot and run dumped apart) plus simpleperf samples for precompiled std and C; text resident after a fling 6.0 -> 3.7 MB on crypto, ~1 MB on daily, CPU unchanged. What is left: (1) the text that stays touched (~1 MB of the 3.7 sits in 64 KB fault-around windows around rarely run, uninstrumented code: drop glue, `core::fmt`, ring); the order file goes stale as code moves and is regenerated by hand. (2) The reader's dex: Media3 ExoPlayer is in every app's reader though only xheavy plays video; moving it to xheavy (a `VideoPlayer` the app gives, `bench-slim-reader-unshipped.patch`) took crypto's classes.dex 1,074 -> 108 KB and PSS -2 to -4 MB, but most installs of that build then ran the reader's `Layers.sample` -> `Player.replay` in ART's interpreter (nterp, `ResolveFieldWithAccessChecks`): main thread +55 ms/s; the cause (verification at runtime?) is not found, so it is not shipped. (3) Rust heap ~11 MB live on crypto (the baked market's values 4.7 MB: 48 numbers a coin at 16 bytes; layout leaves, realized rows, text caches) and mimalloc ~2.6 MB over scudo for the same blocks. (4) xheavy: exact2's decoded-picture cache (8 viewports, ~107 MB at 1260x2650) is most of its 133 MB of mimalloc pages, beside the reader's GPU copies of what is drawn; `imgvp=2` took PSS 449 -> 385 but decodes again on the way back. (5) Graphics is counted in whole swapchain buffers, ~7 MB each (1260x2800x4 shared with SurfaceFlinger), and an app holds 1–4 of them depending on how its first frames queued: daily's PSS moves by 7 MB between runs of one build, so its comparisons need several rounds (the warm Starred/Settings pages cost ~3 MB of heap; the rest of their 45 -> 61 MB was buffers).
- **Android steady-state CPU, what is left after `android/cpu`** (2026-10-05, merged as 98b178bd2; Pixel 10a: the exact thread −3 to −20%, fps unchanged, still more total CPU than Views at every app and speed). (1) Done on `android/cpu2` (the text cache walks only when a budget could be over). (2)–(4): see the `android/cpu3` entry above. (5) crypto at rest: the bench's source clones the 5,000-row list each 10 Hz tick (~26 ms/s) and `update_data` diffs it (~15 ms/s); a source that says which rows changed (as the iOS crypto note above asks) removes both. (6) Per-row construction: rows are now rebound, not built, in the Canvas host (LLP 1078, `android/reuse`: the `exact` thread −17 to −28% on easy at 6k–24k, −8 to −16% on crypto, heavy and xheavy within noise). What a rebound row still costs is its content: layout of its changed text, binding evaluation (crypto's chart `points`), recording it again, and for heavy/xheavy shaping and pictures; easy's process CPU ties Views at 24k and is ~8% over at 12k, crypto's ~60% over. Rows holding a refused leaf (canvas, map, video, web view, inner list) are still built; the Apple and web hosts do not opt in yet. (7) scudo was ~60 ms/s; mimalloc 2.x is now the default (2026-10-05, dd34e6948; `EXACT_MALLOC=system` to compare).
- **Android's Canvas host on an owner thread: what is owed** (2026-10-04, LLP 1072 on Android; the reader is `~/bench/lists/exact/android/canvas/.../Owner.kt`, outside the repo; `owner=0` is the old main-thread host). exact2 runs on an `exact` HandlerThread from boot (its looper takes executor and image fds; its Choreographer the turns); each op stream is copied and published, the main thread ingests and replays it, and a scroll translates the feed's group on main at once (the group holds every mounted row, so the mounted window is the coverage). Fling fps now ties or beats Views at every speed on all four lists on a Pixel 10 Pro XL, a Pixel 10a and a Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (xheavy 24k: 101–106 vs Views 79–83 on the Pixels, 74 vs 63 on the Nothing; owner=0 60–71). Owed: (1) CPU: more ms/s than owner=0 on light lists (easy +10–25%), fewer Mcycles on the Pixel, more on the Nothing; the exact thread is not boosted as main and RenderThread are (ADPF hint sessions measured no better). (2) Input: a press waits ≤5 ms and a key ≤2 ms for the answer within the frame; a 16 ms wait held frames (9–15 janky per daily scenario); a tab switch's ~14 ms of `pointer_up` arrives a frame or two late (39 vs Views' 16 ms). (3) WebView rows: `WebViewFunctor::drawVk` costs 2.3× Views' per draw (12.7 vs 5.5 ms) inside HWUI's `flush layers`, with `initVk` and shader compiles each draw (Views pays the init too). (4) Memory after a crypto fling on the Pixel: 319 vs 197 MB (graphics 200 vs 153, native heap 76 vs 32, Java 19 vs 6); recycling the stream copies did not move the Java heap. (5) Slicing a collection pass between frames on main (GapWorker-style, `refine(limit, velocity)`) painted every frame and cost fps; the limit plumbing stays for fills.
- **Daily's first detail open, the engine options measured** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL, simpleperf over the first open only, four runs; nothing built). The answer must gain ~3 ms to make the vsync. (a) Laying out and painting only what the viewport shows: ~70% of the detail's body is in view at once (36 lines of 24 in ~700 of ~1,010 points), so the bound is ~30% of layout and paint, ~1.3 ms on the exact thread. (b) Sharing text work between layout and paint: there is none to share, paint reads the paragraphs layout made (`paragraph_for` 0.3% of the frame); text measure is ~9% of the touch, shaping ~6%. (c) Mounting at the press was not attempted: it needs the runner to run an action on a state it can throw away. The touch itself has no one cost: the action and the update ~21%, the commit's layout ~15%, `after_commit` ~18% (collections ~8%, requests ~7%), hit testing, allocation ~9%. Note for whoever returns to this: the detail slides in from x = W, so its first frame shows nothing of it in any pipeline; a response split into "the layer, empty" then "its content" would read ~10 ms on the bench and show the content no sooner than now (the content's frame still begins at ~+15 ms), so it was not built. The reader's half of the fourth pass's colour path is now tied with the app's cross-fade (a recoloured text layer recorded from its kept runs, not by walking its ops: tabs 304 vs 299 ms/s, medians of four).
- **Daily on Android, fourth pass: colour transitions and the first detail open on Parley** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL; the reader's half is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat4/tools-lat/reader-lat4.patch` against the Parley-ready copy). Landed: a `color` transition of a text without inline runs, and a `background-color` transition of a box's own flat background, are played by the Canvas host's reader (`lower.rs` `LOWER_COLOR`; `EXACT_LOWER_COLOR=0` to compare): the engine presents the target, the node is a layer, and its tracks are the colour's straight channels along the engine's own interpolation in eight linear pieces; the reader records that one layer again in each frame's colour. Daily's tabs with the app's plain `color` transition: 444 -> 319 ms/s (the app's opacity cross-fade: 304; pixels at rest the same). Owed there: the 5% over the cross-fade is the layer recorded again each frame (main thread +16 ms/s for two labels: the glyph arrays and font looked up per record; RenderThread +10%); keyframe animations of colours are still sampled by the engine. The first detail open on Parley, 17-22 ms against ~10 for any other tap: on the exact thread dispatch (the action and the mount) 2.0 ms, layout 3.0 (text measure ~60% of it, shaping ~1 ms: shaping ahead would not make the vsync), paint 1.6; the answer is there at +7.5-8.5 ms, 1-3 ms after the vsync; ingest 2.4-4.7 ms (the eight paragraphs' rows 0.7 of it); the frame then 5-8 ms, with no pipeline compile and a glyph upload of 0.4 ms. Tried: the next frame's callbacks wait for the late answer and that frame draws it (first open 17.4-18.8 -> 15.1-16.8 ms, but 3-5 janky frames a run where there were none): not kept. What would close it is not doing the mount and layout inside the tap (a mounted, hidden detail, as Starred and Settings are; or layout skipped for a subtree translated out of view).
- **Daily on Android, third pass: the Pixel 10a** (2026-10-07; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat/tools-lat/reader-lat3.patch` against the reader at 12b07f4, with the daily app's contract). (1) Tabs' CPU (492 vs Views 446 ms/s) was not the phone's: on every phone the tab labels' `color` transition is painted each of its 250 ms' frames (300 paints a run: the exact thread ~105 ms/s, the main thread recording the view again, the RenderThread the whole window); the 10a only showed it because Views is cheap there (446 against 530-550 on the Pro XLs). The app's labels now cross-fade by opacity, which the reader plays (the plain label stepped out once covered; at rest the pixels are the same): tabs 494 -> 286 ms/s on the 10a, 467 -> 294 on a Pro XL. Lowering `color` and `background-color` transitions in the engine (a layer recorded again with the sampled colour, as a dash phase is) would do this for every app. (2) Nav's p90 on the 10a is ordinary taps a frame late, not only the first open: the main thread wakes for the release on a little core (820 MHz), where `colocate` declines, so the exact thread starts 1.3-2.9 ms late on an idle mid core at 357 MHz, and the workload hint reaches the governor 1.4-4.3 ms on (0.5 ms on a Pro XL). The reader keeps the main thread to the fast cores while a press is held (`android_hint::fast_thread`): late taps 14 of 66 -> 7 of 66, nav 11.8/16.7 -> 10.7/15.7 ms (Views 11.5/13.3), tabs 15.5/18.0 -> 11.0/15.8. Not kept: the hint again at each warm poke (no gain); the threads' own `uclamp.min` through `sched_setattr` (refused to an app). Owed: the hint's latency on this phone (the rest of the late taps), and the first detail open (exact thread 7 ms, ingest ending at +18 ms): shaping its paragraphs ahead would save ~4 of ~12 ms, not enough alone to make the first vsync.
- **Daily on Android, second pass** (2026-10-06, Pixel 10 Pro XL; all of it is the reader's and the bench driver's, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat/tools-lat/reader-lat2.patch` against the reader at aaf2f06 and `daily-driver.patch`). (1) Tabs' p90 was the first screen with a blurred rounded rect: a blur mask filter makes its mask on the GPU (offscreen blur passes, three `FillRectOp` and a `CircularRRectOp` pipeline, compiled after an install). The reader now makes each shadow's mask on the CPU once per shape (an A8 bitmap, drawn as nine pieces, the middle stretched; within 5/255 of the GPU blur): tabs 10.6/20.5 -> 10.2/12.1 ms (Views 13.4/18.1). (2) A fade exact2 began after a late frame's vsync (the main thread waited across it for the answer) was sampled at that vsync, opacity 0, so HWUI drew nothing and the dialog's first frame came a vsync later; such a frame samples at the present time: dialog p90 13.3 -> 10.6. (3) The driver dispatched keys inside its Choreographer callback, so a key's own frame began before it and input-to-frame read ~90 ms for Views and for exact's fast keys; keys are now posted after the frame, in all three stacks: type exact 13.6/15.3, Views 14.1/15.6, Compose 13.6/14.8, and CPU 204 vs Views 205 (it read 183 vs 161 before, when Views drew a key in the frame already under way). Owed: the first detail open (dispatch 1.8 + layout 4.3 + paint 1.6 ms on the exact thread at 1.8 GHz, then 2.1 ms of ingest: 17-23 ms; it sets nav's p90 only on a slower phone); a late-answer hint and `notifyWorkloadSpike` did not help (spike: nav 11.3/13.5 -> 15.0/18.2). Typing's exact thread (~33 ms/s) is 44% paint: the textarea shaped again a key (cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache` took shaping 8.8 -> 6.4% of the thread, not kept), every icon's SVG scene resolved again a paint (~9% of paint, `computed_style` clones), mimalloc's purge at delay 0 (~6%); and each key damages the whole window (RenderThread 3.7 vs Views' 3.1 ms a key).
- **Daily input-to-frame on Android after `android/lat`** (2026-10-05; the reader's half is a bench patch, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat/tools-lat/reader-lat.patch` against the reader at 82286d5; `boost=0,place=0,warmhold=0` is the old behaviour). Cause: a 60 ms tap's release met an idle phone. The exact thread ran the release's commit and paint at 400-650 MHz (5-10 ms), so the answer missed the vsync ~5-6 ms after the tap and the response came a frame later; it also woke on an idle core ~1 ms after the post; and the GPU, poked only at the press, was idle again by the release (the response's first submit 1-4.4 ms against Views' ~0.9, whose ripple draws every frame). Landed: an ADPF hint session over the exact and main threads told of a workload increase at each press, release and key (`android_hint`, 1.8-2 GHz); the exact thread colocated on the waiting main thread's core for an answer (~0.4 ms to start); the reader pokes the GPU every third frame while a press is held (≤400 ms). Pixel 10 Pro XL, medians of 3 interleaved, input-to-frame p50/p90 ms before -> after (Views): tabs 17/29 -> 11/20 (13/17), nav 18/22 -> 11/14 (12/13), sheet 16/18 -> 10/13 (11/12), settings 15/18 -> 8/10 (11/16), dialog 16/18 -> 10/12 (11/12); CPU -4 to +1% (type -19%), janky frames, PSS and first content unchanged. Nothing (4a) Pro: tabs 16/43 -> 13/21 (16/32), nav 18/22 -> 16/20 (19/24), sheet 13/20 -> 12/14, settings 9/17 -> 9/11 (janky 18 -> 8), dialog 12/17 -> 12/13; CPU +1 to +5%. Owed: (1) the first visit of a page that draws a blurred rounded rect (Settings' pill and thumb shadows) renders the blur's mask on the GPU: ~4 ms before the first op plus four pipeline lookups (13-22 ms of RenderThread, tabs' p90 on both phones); a palette of blurred shapes drawn offscreen at boot compiled other pipelines (30 ms) and did not help. (2) The first detail screen's layout (8 paragraphs shaped, ~4 ms) and ingest (~3 ms) miss a vsync. (3) `type`'s p90 now reads ~93 ms on the Pixel: a key answered within its 2 ms wait is drawn in the frame it was dispatched in, which the metric skips (the frame began before the key), as it does every key of Views'; to the end of the first RenderThread draw after the key, exact 12.7/14.4 (before 13.6/15.9), Views 7.4/8.6. The driver's keys, dispatched inside a Choreographer callback, are what the metric cannot see.
- **Daily (the mail app) on Android's owner thread: what is owed** (2026-10-05, `android/damage`, Nothing Phone (4a) Pro; the reader's half is a bench patch, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-damage/tools-dmg/reader-damage.patch`). Landed: a touch down that changes nothing is no frame (`canvas/stream.rs`: a stream whose drawing is the reader's and that defines nothing); a press wakes the GPU offscreen (the reader's `Warm`, a 1x1 renderer of its own), so a release's response no longer pays 7-10 ms of cold GPU; the reader retains the view's drawing when a stream's top level is the last one's (kept rows recorded again into their predecessor's node, changed groups only) and bounds each animated layer's node (clipped), so a toggle damages its row, not the window; `scroll > column > rows` keeps the column's children as rows. Janky frames, exact vs Views, 3 runs interleaved: tabs 7/6, nav 2/19, sheet 3/12, settings 14/3, dialog 3/13, type 71/99 (before: 9/6, 12/21, 12/12, 10/2, 12/8, 66/102). Owed: (1) settings: what is left is SurfaceFlinger's, not the app's: a first frame after ~200 ms idle presents a vsync late ("Display HAL", then "Unknown Jank" and "SurfaceFlinger Scheduling"), 1-5 such per run (each three janky frames) against Views' 0-1; not damage (retain=0 the same), not the release's wait (wait=0 the same), not a window frame at the press (worse). (2) Tabs p90 latency (44-61 vs Views 42) is the first visit of Starred (the runner mounts the list, 17-21 ms in `exact touch`), and in about half the runs with the press warm-up one frame after it takes 30-35 ms of GPU (never with warm=0); unexplained. (3) Colour transitions (`background-color`, `color`) are still painted every frame: the tab labels' 250 ms redraw the window (the top level changes); lowering them, or a top level split into nodes, would make those frames partial. (4) The type scenario's ~95 ms for Views is the metric's (the driver dispatches keys inside a Choreographer callback).
- **Glass morph, parked** (2026-10-02, Charlie: revisit after native buttons; [LLP 1053.000.000.001](llp/1053.000.000.001-glass-morph.rfc.md)). A ghost glass on the motion engine's clock, springing from a leaving glass's box to an entering one's within a glass group (SwiftUI's `glassEffectID`). Its status line lists what the two reviews require: glass separated from content on both platforms, per-commit trees in the Rust host's commit loop, timestamped curve starts, presented geometry, source isolation, cancellation shared by both hosts.
- **Native buttons as menu invokers** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1069.011.000](llp/1069.011.000-native-buttons-everywhere.rfc.md) D5, both reviews of its r2). A native button with an opening `popovertarget`/`commandfor` is refused (one that only closes, a confirmation's row, is admitted): its activation (an own or ancestor handler, the menu's opening, a confirmation, Enter/Space, shortcuts, the agent) needs one routine, and today `Presenter.press` opens the macOS menu before any revalidation (`PresenterMac.swift:684-689`) and the iOS deferred provider and `openConfirmation` press only the invoker's own handler (`MenusIOS.swift:118-129`, `:248`). The reviews' requirements: resolve and press the target once, revalidate after the synchronous batch, then open exactly once from the control; no second provider run under `presentMenu`; agent mode keeps the painted popover.
- **Native buttons, what is owed** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1069.011](llp/1069.011-native-buttons.rfc.md) §7; its contexts and row reuse are [LLP 1069.011.000](llp/1069.011.000-native-buttons-everywhere.rfc.md)). Raster images and subtitles in a face. A real-touch drive of Messages' native swipe actions on a simulator (a swipe that reveals UIKit's actions, then a tap on each; a swipe and a scroll starting on a native body button), owed by D10: at landing the Mac's screen was locked and the agent's held contact reached no window; XCTests cover the actions' faces, colours, presses and the body button's touch cancel.
- **Contract after the apps, what is left** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1035.005.000](llp/1035.005.000-contract-after-the-apps.rfc.md) §3 "As built"). Stages 1–3 landed (`then` on the JavaScript target's clock, RealWorld's redirects as `then`, `contract` blocks refused, Caltrain's formatters as its own `fn`s, inferred `writes`, `provide` sections, records and action `let`, Fieldnotes on records). Left: Messages adopts `then` for a new conversation (the chat-demo Messages, on main since 8c1a551be, took the D1 and D9 rewrites in the merge that brought it); D6's `asks`/`rows` and its exemplar fixes (Messages' per-frame `time` argument first); D10 after a recount; stage 4 (D7b, D4a, D11, D5), each with its consumer. Outside the repo, Interview, Weird Castle and the Bluesky copy need the D1 command and the D9 script (each in its commit's message) before they build against main; so does any Contract source written before today.
- **Conformance after the Messages migration and SF Symbols** (2026-10-02, found landing LLP 1035.005.000). Messages' `swiped` now agrees on all three (fix/health6: the boot's own scroll offsets fire no `scroll` on Linux, as on the page), and `messages --build --linux --strict` compared 4/4 on 2026-10-04 with no local AI service running. Left: `synthetic-symbols`' screenshots differed in 5.53% of pixels; `conform.mjs`'s `LINUX_APART` still names the legacy app's `tap conversation-maya`.
- **Canonical Messages chat-demo fidelity** (2026-09-30). `apps/messages` now ports Expo's `chat-demo`. Remaining source-measured differences are recorded in `apps/messages/README.md`: longest-line balloon fitting, independent-axis send deformation and tail retraction, UIKit's keyboard-overlapping Add popover, and long-history virtualization. A side-by-side Expo recording and physical keyboard/gesture drive are still needed to establish pixel and motion parity.
- **Glass groups, what is owed** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1053.000.000](llp/1053.000.000-glass-groups.rfc.md) §5, built on `lane/glass-group`). (1) The press: interactive glass swelling across the spacing into a neighbour (Messages' inbox bar is grouped at `glassGroup=8` for it), seen on a phone or with the driver's held contact, which moves the Mac's pointer. (2) From James's prototype: `auto` spacing from the row's `gap`, and a morph when a member is replaced. (3) Whether a `style` class may carry host-policy props (`backgroundMaterial`, `glassGroup`): today "a style holds style rows only", where James's style-row spelling could be put in a class. (4) A UIKit glass made another way (a stock glass button) must register with `GlassGroups` to be isolated.
- **macOS: a focused field in a glass bar draws its text above its placeholder** (2026-10-02, found driving Messages' inbox search with `scripts/agent.mjs macos … "type search Al" "screenshot … window"`). The typed "Al" sits about 12 pt above where "Search" was, with the placeholder's tail still showing behind it, with or without a glass group. Not seen on iOS or the web.
- **A folded text's element is still styled: two more styled elements a grid row than Solid's** (2026-10-02, LLP 1007.001 §5). A lone plain text is its box's content now (`display: contents`, the box a block), so the grid's layout tree is Solid's size (create 10,000: 220,022 against 220,033 layout nodes), but its element stays and is style-recalculated: 170,003 against 150,003 elements. Dropping the element too means the JS runtime writes a folded text's string into its parent, the agent's tree reads the node from the plan rather than an element, and the region anchors around it move; measure what the 20,000 recalculations cost first (create 10,000, mobile: style 354 against Solid's 289 ms).
- **A GPU canvas's drawables after its layer dies** (2026-09-30, the rest lane). The Extra Heavy shader rows' purgeable GPU memory grew with the rows passed because Core Animation lets a dead `CAMetalLayer`'s drawables go late: an iPad's 19-kind fling made 37–38 layers with at most two or three alive, and the purgeable ledger held 100–121 MB at the peak. Pooling the layers (LLP 1068 §0.4: 12 made, three spares kept) took the peak 322 → 288 MB (medians of three rounds; SwiftUI's 319 is the bar). What is left: the spares' count against rows turning over faster than the pool refills (six spares measured next), and a canvas that leaves the screen still holds its layer and its three drawables until its row is retired — a target lent only while on screen (`Module::attach`/`detach`, kept on `perf/canvas-pool`) would take the shader-only feed's 12–14 alive layers down to the four on screen.
- **The crypto list at rest, after the app's fix** (2026-09-30, iPhone 13 Pro Max, `~/bench/cryptobench/results/rest-trace/iphone-{svgi,uikit}`). The SVG port formatted every changed coin's 48-point `points` string in its data source each tick, off-screen coins included: 54 of 117 ms/s at rest (`Coin::value` -> `points`, Rust float formatting). SwiftUI and UIKit build a row's path when it shows, so this was the app, not exact2. `exact-svgi` keeps `series: list<number>` and maps it in the view (LLP 1017.003): rest 92 -> 65 ms/s over three rounds (UIKit 22), footprint 27.2 -> 23.5 MB (UIKit 29.4). The traced 63 ms/s that remains (UIKit 27) breaks down as: the port's source 7 (cloning the 5,000-row list and formatting the price); per-tick work proportional to the list, about 10 (the runner's `update_data` 3.7, dropping the replaced rows 3.0, the clone, `compare::same`); layout 3; commit 4; the host's apply and collection flush about 16 (`Frames.tick` outside the runner); UIKit and Core Animation 18. A source that could say which rows changed would take away the per-list part.
- **Signposts cost nothing measurable unless something records them** (2026-09-30). An `OSSignposter` begin/end pair costs about 0.4 us with nothing recording, well under 1 ms/s at our rates. Under Time Profiler, whose kdebug session makes each emit expensive, exact2's own intervals (`scrolled`, `pump-*`, `apply`, `text-raster-*`) cost 17-29 ms/s on the iPhone. So traces overstate exact2 by that much against SwiftUI and UIKit; subtract it when comparing. Not gated. The iPhone 13 Pro Max also logs CFPrefs lookups at debug level (about 20-50 ms/s of `os_log` in every app's traces, SwiftUI's and UIKit's included), which is where the "48 ms/s of logging on main" came from. Safe-area and layout-margin updates cost 4-5 ms/s in exact2 against 3-4 in SwiftUI and UIKit: not worth a change.
- **Agent drives, what is left** (2026-09-30, [LLP 1012.001.000](llp/1012.001.000-trustworthy-agent-drives.rfc.md)): stages 1 and the ruled parts of 2 landed (stale builds refused, film, 420×900 everywhere, source maps on every host, the glue tests in the async lane). Left: D1b the web build marker records its inputs, so the web's staleness warning can refuse; D7 one grammar table for the driver's CLI; D8's rows as authored + computed under the runner's names, `font_family` by name, the page's view numbering and rounding; D10 LLP 1012's crash log to `issues/`.
- **iPhone Duo, what is still owed** (2026-09-30, trimmed 2026-10-02 after LLP 1008's sweep and LLP 1078): rotation and Split View on the Duo, which the 27.1 beta's simulator does not honour from CoreDevice (`devicectl device orientation set` changes nothing; Device Hub rotates through an accessibility path) — re-run `bun scripts/smoke.mjs duo` on a later beta or a released phone; the simulator as found on 2026-10-02 is portrait (inner 669×951, a horizontal division), so the two-across layout is exercised only through `prefer segments 2x1`. The sample host's scene-lifecycle launch failure, the `UIScreen.main` reads and the reserved-region evaluation are done (LLP 1008 §"Scene and display ownership").

- **A minimal iOS embed weighs 4.1–4.7 MB with every compiler lever pulled, not 1** (2026-09-30, measured: a one-text-node plan over `exact_apple::host!` with an empty data source, linked into a UIKit `main.swift` against a 73 KB UIKit floor, arm64 iPhone, `-dead_strip`, `strip`; harness and link maps in `~/bench/embedsize`, outside the repo). Today's shapes: the production profile (`release`: opt-level 3, thin LTO; `swift build -c release`) is 7.23 MB over the floor; a real app's `apple-dev` archive (Type Tour's) 8.45, of which wasmi 1.0 MB, linked into every app through `exact_logic::configured!` for the swappable data module (LLP 1029.000) whether or not the app swaps one, the app's data and plan 0.7, the kernel 1.33 unoptimized for size. The ladder on the probe: opt-level z + thin LTO 5.66 → fat LTO 5.42 → Swift `-Osize` 4.92 → `-Xfrontend -internalize-at-link -enforce-exclusivity=unchecked` 4.65 (the export penalty vanishes with it: ordinary exports cost 1.2 MB before, 5 KB after) → nightly `-Zbuild-std=std,panic_abort` with `optimize_for_size` 4.34 → `-Cpanic=immediate-abort` 4.22 (the backtrace machinery was 161 KB) → `-Zlocation-detail=none -Zfmt-debug=none` 4.21 → `-Xfrontend -disable-reflection-metadata` 4.09 (not free: `String(describing:)` of an enum then prints its type name, `State`, not its case, `ready`; the sample hosts and the agent print states that way). opt-level s in place of z costs +0.8–0.9 MB. What the 4.09 holds: ExactKit 1.98 MB (core presenter files ~0.8; Canvas, SVG, GPU, video, web, native modules, pickers, collections, navigation, menus, modals, swipe ~0.7; generic specializations and metadata ~0.2), Rust 1.75 (taffy 222 KB of which grid 161; runner 221; kernel 220; exact_apple 145; core, alloc and std 280; motion 30; markdown 28; plan 28; textflow and unicode-linebreak 41; url, idna and ibex2 46; route 11; raster and canvas 14), linker-synthesized stubs and unwind 0.14. The stable flags-only build (fat LTO, z, `-Osize -internalize-at-link`) boots and paints on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator; the nightly one is checked below it. Why 1 MB is out of reach without a different design: the feature code is linked by dispatch, not by use (every op handler is reachable from `Presenter.apply` and `NodeView`; every kernel row's grammar from the plan decoder), so removing it means separate artifacts a consumer links (CLAUDE.md's rule), and the core beneath — Swift ~1.0 MB, Rust ~1.3 — is still 2–3× the budget. Owed: whether `-Osize` and opt-level z cost frames on the iPad benchmarks, before either becomes the iOS production profile. Linking by use on Apple, Swift included, is LLP 1047.001 (its §6 is the order of work).
- **Considered and deferred: an edge fires only over a measured band** (2026-09-29, LLP 1010 §6.5, the lead's ruling). `reachstart`/`reachend` fire by the estimate today: a report whose window reaches the edge row by estimated heights dispatches, and only re-arming needs the overscan band measured. Requiring the band measured to fire too would stop an under-estimate from paging at load. The feed benchmark's case is closed without it (a realistic estimate plus `initial-item-count`). It costs rewriting about 20 edge tests (`contract/cli/tests/it/collection_edges.rs`, `collection_edge_async.rs`), which send estimate-only reports, and the same change in `list.js`.
- **Render host CPU a page against Solid and Svelte** (2026-09-29, the SSR-cost lane, LLP 1048.000 D10). RealWorld's `/` is ~4.3 ms of CPU a page on this Mac under load (Solid ~0.8), and the grid benchmark's 1,000-row `/` ~125 ms (Solid 24 on the same load), after this lane's commits. Measured by A/B, not by profile alone: (1) Apple's transport was ~30% of it; the render host now uses rustls on macOS too (Charlie, 2026-09-29; LLP 1048.000 D10), −17–18%. (2) The fresh Hermes realm is ~14% (`EXACT_RENDER_REALMS=warm`'s saving): Runtime creation (its internal bytecode, the global object) and teardown; this Hermes has no heap snapshot, and a realm's module state lives in closures no reflection reaches, so a proven reset needs an engine snapshot or a bake that proves a module keeps no mutable state. (3) The kernel's apply and the projection are gone for pages the plan allows (LLP 1048.004 stage 1, 2026-09-30: RealWorld −18%, grid −49%); the rest is the render itself: the tree's realization and bindings (the plan VM), the kernel's apply (style validation, inheritance, the testId index), the projection and its CSS, JSON across the Hermes boundary. Reaching Solid's cost needs a render that writes the document from the plan and the settled state without the kernel (precompiled static fragments, as Solid and Svelte do), plus (1) and (2). At concurrency 64 the server's defaults (`--renders 4 --queue 32`) answer most requests 503 at once, so a CPU-per-page number there counts cheap refusals unless statuses are checked.
- **Fieldnotes' `backupNotes` on the JS target is the TypeScript one** (2026-09-29, LLP 1071 §7 "Files and storage"). The wasm target's Rust source owns it (`RUST_SOURCES`, `build_mixed_with`); the JS build takes a Rust module only from `rust.module` or a `contract::bake` web build script, so a `build_mixed_with` app without `rust.module` gets no Rust module and its TypeScript answers. A generated module over the app's composed Rust source would carry it.
- **The wasm target on the web: what is left of LLP 1071 §7's retirement order** (2026-09-29, "Retiring the wasm target on the web"): the router sweep's in-document reboot, not run on the JS target (`exact.reload`, which the JS target, compiling one plan, has no counterpart for); and a conformance reference without the wasm page, whose first step is built (§7, "A conformance reference without the wasm page": `conform.mjs --linux` in the lane, 157 Linux steps equal; routing and pinned layout/pixel baselines wait on §8 question 7) before the wasm web host leaves everything but games and conformance. (The JS agent's gaps closed on 2026-09-29: `smoke.mjs web` passes on the JS target, fixtures as per-plan JS builds.)
- **Agent-mode storage differs between the Linux host and the page** (2026-09-29, found by `conform.mjs --linux`, LLP 1071 §7). With no `--storage`, the web page refuses app storage in agent mode (`host/web/storage-environment.js`), so Messages' `savedComposer`, `chat` and `change` requests fail there and opening a thread writes nothing. On the Linux host the same drive's data sources store and answer (the thread is marked read, the draft thread is saved). A drive should get the same storage on every host; until it does, the Linux comparison of Messages stops at its first tap (`LINUX_APART`).
- **LLP 1072 stage 5 on macOS: fills off the main thread** (2026-09-30, the mac lane; not started). After the heavy-leaf hold, what exact2 still loses to SwiftUI on the Extra Heavy feed on bones (M4, 60 Hz) is the owner thread on a synchronous host: process CPU 585 vs 549 ms/s and busy 6.94 vs 6.79 ms/frame over the fling; the owner is 29/31/46/83 ms/s at 1k/3k/6k/24k pt/s (at 1k–6k: batch decode and prepared values 10–16, the runner realizing rows 8–12, the host commit 6–9, layout 2–5, text measurement 1–2), and main is blocked for the same time, about 0.6 ms a frame at 1k–6k and 1.4 at 24k. In the inbox at 24k–48k main's own CPU is at SwiftUI's level (424–443 vs 417–441 ms/s) and busy is 8.4–9.1 vs 7.1–7.4 ms/frame: main's 7.2 plus 1.6–2 waiting for the owner's build, over a 120 Hz frame's 8.3 where SwiftUI is under it. Stage 5 takes that wait off main and no CPU. The switch is one static on macOS (`ExactSession.asyncFills`); `PresenterMac`'s pump needs the latency lead and the spent-turn deferral `ScrollPumpIOS` has; the first step is an A/B with the switch flipped, on a visible window at 120 Hz (no such Mac was usable on 2026-09-30: silver's series ran occluded, the M1's CPU speed swings); it is a threading change (PROGRAM rule 6) in shared files. The batch decode on the owner is the one CPU item in it.
- **macOS after the heavy-leaf hold** (2026-09-30, `perf/mac2`, bones M4 60 Hz, the Extra Heavy fling): exact2 against SwiftUI is fps 59.4 vs 55.6, busy 7.0 vs 6.8 ms/frame, process CPU 587–595 vs 549, main 348–354 vs 347, peak 807 vs 1043 MB, rest 166/67 vs 405/331. Measured and not built: the owner thread is +50 ms/s by itself (collection feedback 30, batch decode 10); AppKit's tracking-area and cursor-rect walk under the scroll view is 25 ms/s of main against SwiftUI's 9 (its own walk over every NSView: fewer views, not the `updateTrackingAreas` override, which measured nothing); layer display is diffuse per-view overhead (66 vs 19 ms/s) that flat leaves and view reuse (both iOS-only) would cut; the inbox's apply of message rows is 153 ms/s (children 51, style 29, props 29); small labels (under 16,384 device pixels, most one-line labels at 1x) still paint on main; peak memory's default malloc zone is +230 MB over SwiftUI's, unattributed. The 120 Hz number is unmeasured on a healthy Mac: every silver series of 09-28/29 ran occluded (`window: visible 0`), and the M1 MacBook Pro's CPU speed swings 3–5x with other jobs' bursts (`~/bench/xheavy-mac/SPEC.md`, 2026-09-30).
- **Watch: the canvas smoke's macOS page-3 state fixtures** (2026-09-28): `fx-accumulate`, `fx-stack`, `fx-reset`, `fx-throws` and `fx-resize` failed now and then at step 1+ on a Retina Mac and on the mini, on lanes that touch canvas and lanes that do not (coresize), each time looking like the frame before (the backdrop tap not yet shown). The parity capture on macOS is the window server's picture (`screenshot … window`), which showed the last *composited* frame; it now commits the transaction and waits two refreshes first (`perf/webview-direct`), and every `screenshot` waits for Canvas 2D replays not yet shown (LLP 1056 §8.3). Since then three local runs and the mini's run passed the whole canvas smoke, and the mini's SVG smoke failed only `fx-pattern-hatch` at 1x (4.43/255). The failures the mini's note blamed on 1x were mostly this stale frame. If the set fails again, the cause is elsewhere.
- **LLP 1077's visual properties grew every wasm core ~18 KiB, none of it linked by use** (2026-10-02, the M5 mini's `metrics.mjs --long` on d09bfd087 against b65f6fe6 a merge earlier: Caltrain 309 → 327, RealWorld 303 → 322, video-player 247 → 265 KiB brotli-11; code ~18–19 KiB raw in `core`, data ~13–14 KiB). The rows LLP 1077 added (`box-shadow` lists, conic gradients and layers, `mask-image`, `text-shadow`, `corner-shape`, `-webkit-text-stroke`, `rotate`'s axis, `-exact-symbol-palette`) parse through generated style code every core reaches, so an app that uses none of them carries all of their grammars. Link them by use as `gradients` (2edce3faf) and `grid` (e5d8707d, dcbfa8c0) are: the parser reached on wasm only through a registered link, set by a `host/web-capabilities` capability when the plan binds the row, a plan that binds it unlinked refused at boot. The shipped app.js barely moved (69 → 70 KiB raw), and the wasm cores are no longer gated, so this is size for games and fixtures, not for web apps.
- **Standing rule: a new optional web capability links by use** (2026-09-28, Charlie's ruling (a) on the web cores' growth). A feature an app may not use — a device request, a host command, a style or prop family — reaches the web core only through what the generated entry links (`Linked` / `HostLinks` / `RunnerLinks` / `DeviceLinks`), detected at build from the plan (`exact_runner::uses`: its rows, tags and `Command`s) or, for a data module's requests, from the bake's grant ceiling; a plan that uses it unlinked is refused at boot by name. The gate (since 2026-10-02, Charlie: gate what ships) is `metrics.mjs --long`'s app.js budgets, the JS target each app ships (Caltrain 19, RealWorld 30, video-player 9 KiB brotli-11); the wasm cores (what games, conformance and fixtures build) keep reported reference numbers (310 / 304 / 249 KiB) that print "over" and are not filed. Smaller is still better for both, so a lane measures its app cores and app.js before and after and says the difference in its commit. Precedents: Canvas 2D stage 2, `format`, `materials`, `backdrop`, `auth`, `share`, `documents`, `picker`, `text_transform`, `effects` (filter, clip-path), `animations`, `gradients`. Numbers in core crates read and print through `exact-num`, never `str::parse::<f64>` or `{}` of a float: either one brings back core's tables (~12 KiB and ~4.5 KiB).
- **CSS `filter` on an HTML box draws unfiltered on Apple and Linux** (2026-09-29, found by `~/bench/features` F3; an SVG `g`'s works everywhere). The kernel accepts the row on any node and only the web lowers it for a box; the native hosts ignore it without a word. Implementing it on Apple means rendering the box's subtree (`CALayer.render(in:)`, as an SVG island does) into the filter region, running `exact-svg-raster`'s chain over it (the kernel's `Resolver::filter` over the border box) and showing that in its place, re-rendered whenever anything under the box changes or animates, with hit testing left on the real views. Until then, at least say so once, as iOS does for `mix-blend-mode`. Charlie's call between building it and refusing it on those hosts.
